#!/usr/bin/env python3
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""LLM  ->  Slide IR.  Model-agnostic by construction.

STATUS: the generative pipeline (``build_system_prompt``, ``generate_outline``,
``fill_slide``, ``generate_deck``) and ``CliWorkerClient`` (headless claude/codex CLI,
no API key -- see the headless-worker-integration Work Order) are implemented. The
reference API-key clients below (``ClaudeClient``, ``OpenAIClient``, ``GeminiClient``)
still raise NotImplementedError -- out of scope; the user declined that path.

The single invariant this module exists to protect
[REDACTED]
**The model never emits PPTX, OOXML, DrawingML, coordinates, fonts or colours.**
It emits schema-valid IR: a ``layout_id`` plus placeholder content. Everything visual
comes from the template and the renderer. If you ever find yourself adding an `x`, `y`,
`rgb` or `font_size` field to the IR to make a slide look right, the bug is in the
renderer or the template -- fix it there.

Why structured output and not free-text-then-parse
[REDACTED]
"Return JSON" in a system prompt yields JSON *most* of the time, and the failures are
the expensive kind: a stray markdown fence, a trailing comma, a hallucinated field. Every
supported provider can constrain output to a schema, so we use that and get a hard
guarantee instead of a soft one:

    Claude  -> tool use / `tools=[{input_schema: SCHEMA}]`, force with tool_choice
    OpenAI  -> Structured Outputs / `response_format={type: json_schema, strict: true}`
    Gemini  -> `response_mime_type="application/json"` + `response_schema`

All three consume the *same* ``ir/schema.json``. That is the entire point of having an IR:
the schema is the neutral contract, and the model behind it is a swappable implementation
detail. Adding a provider must not require touching the renderer, the prompts, or the IR.

Two-stage generation (see prompts/)
[REDACTED]
    stage 1  10_outline_storyboard.md  topic + sources -> deck narrative, one line/slide
    stage 2  20_slide_fill.md          one slide's outline entry -> that slide's IR

Stage 2 runs per-slide and is parallelisable. The split exists because a single call that
has to hold the whole deck's narrative *and* respect 30 different per-placeholder
character budgets does neither job well: it drifts on structure and overflows on length.
Separating "what story do we tell" from "what words go in this box" fixes both.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import math
import os
import re
import shutil
import subprocess
import tempfile
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Protocol

from jsonschema import validators
from jsonschema.exceptions import ValidationError

from ..ir.models import (
    Bullets,
    Callout,
    Cards,
    Deck,
    DeckMeta,
    Heading,
    IR_VERSION,
    IRValidationError,
    Paragraph,
    Slide,
    Steps,
    Table,
    load_schema,
)

#: This module's project root -- ``tasks/AI_Powered_PPT`` -- three levels above
#: ``src/aippt/adapters/ai_adapter.py``. Same convention ``canvas/pin.py`` and
#: ``tools/calibrate.py`` use for the same directory depth. ``prompts/`` and
#: ``reference/`` are data files, not package resources: the repo-root ``.gitignore``
#: keeps them out of a fresh checkout of ``tasks/*``, so this resolves correctly in a
#: full working copy but not inside a staging clone that only carries ``src/``.
_PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3]
_PROMPTS_DIR = _PROJECT_ROOT / "prompts"
_REFERENCE_DIR = _PROJECT_ROOT / "reference"


class LLMClient(Protocol):
    """The seam. Claude, Codex and Gemini are interchangeable *behind this*.

    One method, because one method is all the pipeline needs. Anything provider-specific
    (auth, retries, rate limits, base URLs) lives inside the implementation, never leaks
    into the caller.
    """

    def complete_json(
        self,
        *,
        system: str,
        user: str,
        schema: dict[str, Any],
        max_tokens: int = 8000,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Return a dict the provider has *already* constrained to ``schema``.

        Implementations MUST use the provider's native structured-output mechanism.
        An implementation that calls ``json.loads`` on free text is not conforming --
        it silently reintroduces exactly the failure mode this interface removes.
        """
        ...


@dataclass
class GenerationResult:
    deck: Deck
    attempts: int
    repaired: bool          # True if a retry was needed -- track this; a rising rate
                            # means the prompt or the budget has drifted from reality.
    token_usage: dict[str, int]


def build_system_prompt(manifest: dict[str, Any], catalog_md: str) -> str:
    """Assemble the system prompt from ``prompts/00_system_deck_author.md`` + the catalog.

    The catalog (``reference/layout_catalog.md``) is injected *whole*. It carries the two
    things the model cannot guess and must not invent:

      * the exact set of legal ``layout_id`` values, and
      * the per-placeholder **content budget** ("max 6 bullets x 9 words", "title <= 42
        Korean chars").

    Injecting the budget is what makes the model self-limit. Without it the model writes
    to a length that reads well in chat and overflows a 4-inch box every time -- and since
    python-pptx cannot autofit, that overflow is permanent and invisible until render.

    Note the budget is stated in BOTH Latin and Korean character counts, because a Hangul
    glyph is roughly twice the advance width of a Latin one. A model told "60 characters"
    with no script qualifier will happily overflow a Korean slide by 2x.
    """
    del manifest  # the caller already rendered catalog_md from this manifest; see _load_catalog_md
    template = (_PROMPTS_DIR / "00_system_deck_author.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    return template.replace("{{LAYOUT_CATALOG}}", catalog_md)


def _load_catalog_md() -> str:
    """The pre-rendered ``reference/layout_catalog.md`` -- read from disk rather than
    regenerated via ``analyze_pptx_master.render_catalog(manifest)``, so this module never
    needs a ``python-pptx`` dependency of its own."""
    return (_REFERENCE_DIR / "layout_catalog.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8")


def _outline_schema(manifest: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """The stage-1 structured-output schema. ``layout_id`` is a closed ``enum`` built from
    the manifest's own layouts -- an invented layout is rejected by the provider's own
    structured-output mechanism, before it ever reaches ``validate_against_manifest``.
    """
    layout_ids = sorted({
        lay["layout_id"]
        for master in manifest.get("masters", [])
        for lay in master.get("layouts", [])
    })
    slide_entry = {
        "type": "object",
        "required": ["id", "layout_id", "title", "intent", "key_points"],
        "additionalProperties": False,
        "properties": {
            "id": {"type": "string", "minLength": 1},
            "layout_id": {"type": "string", "enum": layout_ids},
            "title": {"type": "string", "minLength": 1, "maxLength": 200},
            "intent": {"type": "string", "minLength": 1},
            "key_points": {"type": "array", "items": {"type": "string"}},
            "source_refs": {"type": "array", "items": {"type": "string"}},
        },
    }
    return {
        "type": "object",
        "required": ["slides", "rationale"],
        "additionalProperties": False,
        "properties": {
            "slides": {"type": "array", "minItems": 1, "items": slide_entry},
            "rationale": {"type": "string"},
        },
    }


def generate_outline(client: LLMClient, topic: str, sources: list[str],
                     manifest: dict[str, Any], target_slides: int = 12) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    """Stage 1: topic + source material -> a storyboard.

    Returns one entry per slide::

        {"id": "slide-03", "layout_id": "...", "title": "...",
         "intent": "why this slide exists, one line",
         "key_points": ["...", "..."]}

    The outline is deliberately NOT full IR: no blocks, no prose. Its job is narrative
    arc and layout selection -- deciding that slide 3 is a section divider and slide 4 is
    a comparison table. Cheap to generate, cheap to review, cheap to regenerate.

    ``layout_id`` is chosen here, at deck level, where the model can see the whole shape
    and avoid emitting nine consecutive bullet slides.
    """
    system_prompt = build_system_prompt(manifest, _load_catalog_md())
    template = (_PROMPTS_DIR / "10_outline_storyboard.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    user_prompt = (
        template.replace("{{TOPIC}}", topic)
                .replace("{{SOURCES}}", "\n".join(sources))
                .replace("{{TARGET_SLIDES}}", str(target_slides))
    )
    result = client.complete_json(system=system_prompt, user=user_prompt,
                                  schema=_outline_schema(manifest))
    return result["slides"]


def _find_layout(manifest: dict[str, Any], layout_id: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """``layout_id`` -> its full manifest entry. Raises plain ``CliWorkerError`` (not
    ``CliWorkerSchemaError``) when the name doesn't exist at all: that is a structural
    defect no repair prompt can fix, since the outline schema's own ``enum`` should have
    made it unreachable in the first place.
    """
    for master in manifest.get("masters", []):
        for lay in master.get("layouts", []):
            if lay["layout_id"] == layout_id:
                return lay
    raise CliWorkerError(
        f"layout_id {layout_id!r} does not exist in this manifest's layouts -- "
        "no repair prompt can fix a layout the template does not have."
    )


def _format_layout_budget(layout: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    """One layout's placeholder budgets, in the same per-placeholder shape
    ``analyze_pptx_master.render_catalog`` uses for the full catalog -- but scoped to just
    THIS layout, which is the point of stage 2's narrower prompt (see module docstring).
    """
    lines = [f"Layout `{layout['layout_id']}` ({layout['semantic_role']}):"]
    placeholders = layout.get("placeholders") or []
    if not placeholders:
        lines.append("- this layout has no fillable placeholders (decoration only)")
        return "\n".join(lines)
    for ph in placeholders:
        b = ph.get("content_budget") or {}
        head = f"- `idx {ph['idx']}` {ph['type']}"
        if not b.get("computable"):
            lines.append(f"{head} -- budget unavailable ({b.get('reason')})")
        elif "max_title_chars_cjk" in b:
            lines.append(
                f"{head} -- max {b['max_title_chars_latin']} latin / "
                f"{b['max_title_chars_cjk']} korean chars, <= {b['recommended_lines']} lines"
            )
        elif "max_bullets" in b:
            lines.append(
                f"{head} -- max {b['max_bullets']} bullets x {b['max_words_per_bullet']} "
                f"words ({b['max_chars_per_bullet_latin']} latin / "
                f"{b['max_chars_per_bullet_cjk']} korean chars per bullet)"
            )
        else:
            lines.append(f"{head} -- {b.get('chars_per_line_latin')} latin chars/line, "
                         f"{b.get('max_lines')} lines")
    return "\n".join(lines)


def _slide_schema() -> dict[str, Any]:
    """``ir/schema.json``'s ``$defs.slide`` lifted to the document root, with the full
    ``$defs`` carried alongside so its internal ``#/$defs/...`` refs still resolve -- a
    JSON Schema ``$ref`` is resolved against the root of whatever document is actually
    being validated, and stage 2 validates one slide, not a whole deck.
    """
    full = load_schema()
    schema = dict(full["$defs"]["slide"])
    schema["$schema"] = full.get("$schema", "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema")
    schema["$defs"] = full["$defs"]
    return schema


def fill_slide(client: LLMClient, outline_entry: dict[str, Any],
               manifest: dict[str, Any], sources: list[str], *,
               repair_note: str = "") -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Stage 2: one outline entry -> one schema-valid slide object.

    The prompt injects ONLY the budget for the layout this slide actually uses -- not the
    whole catalog. Narrowing the context measurably improves adherence: the model is not
    picking a layout any more, it is filling a box whose exact dimensions it has been told.

    ``repair_note``, keyword-only and additive to the original signature: when
    ``generate_deck``'s bounded repair loop re-prompts after a validation failure, the
    specific error goes here and is substituted into ``{{REPAIR_NOTE}}``.
    """
    layout = _find_layout(manifest, outline_entry["layout_id"])
    system_prompt = build_system_prompt(manifest, _load_catalog_md())
    template = (_PROMPTS_DIR / "20_slide_fill.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    repair_block = (
        f"## This slide was rejected -- fix it\n\n{repair_note}" if repair_note else ""
    )
    user_prompt = (
        template.replace("{{OUTLINE_ENTRY}}",
                         json.dumps(outline_entry, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2))
                .replace("{{LAYOUT_ID}}", layout["layout_id"])
                .replace("{{SEMANTIC_ROLE}}", layout["semantic_role"])
                .replace("{{LAYOUT_BUDGET}}", _format_layout_budget(layout))
                .replace("{{SOURCES}}", "\n".join(sources))
                .replace("{{REPAIR_NOTE}}", repair_block)
    )
    return client.complete_json(system=system_prompt, user=user_prompt, schema=_slide_schema())


def _check_layout_matches_entry(slide_dict: dict[str, Any], entry: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    """The outline's layout decision is final. ``fill_slide`` is prompted with exactly one
    layout, but nothing stops the model from answering with a different one -- and content
    validated against one layout's budget, rendered through another's placeholders, is
    exactly the corrupted-but-plausible deck this whole module exists to prevent.
    """
    got = slide_dict.get("layout_id")
    want = entry["layout_id"]
    if got != want:
        raise CliWorkerSchemaError(
            f"slide {entry.get('id')!r}: the outline assigned layout_id {want!r}, but the "
            f"filled slide used {got!r}. Content must be written for the layout it was "
            "assigned -- re-fill it for the correct layout."
        )


def _parse_and_validate_slide(slide_dict: dict[str, Any], manifest: dict[str, Any]) -> Slide:
    """Structural validation JSON Schema cannot express: wraps the slide in a throwaway
    one-slide deck and reuses ``Deck.from_dict`` (ragged table rows, malformed rich text)
    plus ``validate_against_manifest`` (unknown ``layout_id`` -- belt-and-suspenders with
    ``_check_layout_matches_entry``, which catches the same defect earlier and by name).
    """
    throwaway = {
        "ir_version": IR_VERSION,
        "deck": {"title": slide_dict.get("title") or "untitled"},
        "slides": [slide_dict],
    }
    deck = Deck.from_dict(throwaway)
    errors = deck.validate_against_manifest(manifest)
    if errors:
        raise IRValidationError("slides[0].layout_id", "; ".join(errors))
    return deck.slides[0]


def _line_count(text: str, max_chars_per_line: int) -> int:
    """How many line-slots ``text`` costs against a placeholder's per-line character cap --
    the manifest's own rule ("a bullet that wraps consumes another slot"), applied
    literally rather than approximated by a raw character-count sum.
    """
    if not text:
        return 0
    return max(1, math.ceil(len(text) / max_chars_per_line))


def _block_text_items(block: Any) -> list[str]:
    """The text units a line-budget check measures one at a time. Each item consumes at
    least one line slot; a long item consumes ``ceil(len(item) / max_chars_per_line)``.

    ``code``, ``image`` and ``diagram`` blocks are deliberately excluded (empty list):
    they are not budgeted by character count (monospace/fixed-width, area, and Mermaid
    source respectively) -- that is real ``render/fit.py`` geometry work, still
    ``NotImplementedError`` there, and not something this bullet-shaped estimate should
    pretend to check.
    """
    if isinstance(block, Bullets):
        return [item.text.plain for item in block.items]
    if isinstance(block, Steps):
        return [f"{item.title} {item.text.plain if item.text else ''}".strip()
                for item in block.items]
    if isinstance(block, Cards):
        return [f"{item.title} {item.text.plain if item.text else ''}".strip()
                for item in block.items]
    if isinstance(block, Table):
        return [cell.plain for row in block.rows for cell in row]
    if isinstance(block, Paragraph):
        return [block.text.plain]
    if isinstance(block, Callout):
        return [f"{block.title or ''} {block.text.plain}".strip()]
    if isinstance(block, Heading):
        return [block.text]
    return []


def _check_slide_budget(slide: Slide, layout: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    """Local re-implementation of the check ``render/fit.py:check_budget`` still cannot do
    (it is ``NotImplementedError`` -- see that module's docstring). Deliberately simpler
    than the real thing: no word-wrap simulation, no per-character CJK-vs-Latin walk. It
    exists so the repair loop has something to catch an overflowing slide with, not to
    replace ``fit.py`` once it is implemented.

    Title-placeholder fallback, specific to THIS template: ``analyze_pptx_master.
    content_budget()`` only tags a placeholder's budget with ``max_title_chars_cjk`` when
    its ``ph_type`` is TITLE / CENTER_TITLE / VERTICAL_TITLE. Every placeholder in
    ``reference/layout_manifest.json`` is reported as plain ``BODY`` (confirmed directly
    against the manifest), so that tag never appears here and a title-vs-body split has to
    come from somewhere else. Placeholders are listed idx-ascending in the manifest, and
    idx-ascending is also top-to-bottom in every layout in this template (checked against
    each placeholder's ``inches.top``) -- so the first placeholder is used as the title's
    target, budgeted against its own single-line character cap. A template whose analyzer
    output DOES carry a real title tag is still handled correctly above it.

    Both title and body checks budget against the ``_cjk`` figures unconditionally, never
    ``_latin``: ``ir/schema.json``'s own ``deck.language`` doc says a ``"mixed"`` deck (the
    default) budgets against CJK as the pessimistic case, and this pipeline has no
    per-slide language signal to budget against ``_latin`` selectively even if it wanted
    to. Budgeting English content against the Korean cap is stricter than necessary, never
    unsafe -- the wrong direction to be wrong in.
    """
    placeholders = layout.get("placeholders") or []
    if not placeholders:
        return  # blank/decoration-only layout -- nothing to check content against

    title_ph, *body_placeholders = placeholders
    title_budget = title_ph.get("content_budget") or {}
    if title_budget.get("computable"):
        title_cap = title_budget.get("max_title_chars_cjk",
                                     title_budget.get("max_chars_per_bullet_cjk"))
        if title_cap and len(slide.title) > title_cap:
            raise IRValidationError(
                "title",
                f"{len(slide.title)} chars exceeds this layout's {title_cap}-char title "
                f"budget (placeholder idx {title_ph['idx']}) -- cut it or split the slide"
            )

    for i, block in enumerate(slide.blocks):
        if i >= len(body_placeholders):
            break  # schema: overflow blocks fall through to free-floating shapes, unbudgeted
        budget = body_placeholders[i].get("content_budget") or {}
        if not budget.get("computable") or "max_bullets" not in budget:
            continue
        max_bullets = budget["max_bullets"]
        max_chars = budget["max_chars_per_bullet_cjk"]
        total_lines = sum(_line_count(t, max_chars) for t in _block_text_items(block))
        if total_lines > max_bullets:
            raise IRValidationError(
                f"blocks[{i}]",
                f"needs {total_lines} lines but placeholder idx "
                f"{body_placeholders[i]['idx']} holds only {max_bullets} "
                f"(max {max_chars} korean chars per line) -- shorten it or split the slide"
            )


def generate_deck(client: LLMClient, topic: str, sources: list[str],
                  manifest_path: Path, *, max_repairs: int = 2) -> GenerationResult:
    """Full two-stage generation with a bounded repair loop.

    The loop, and why it is bounded:

        1. outline  -> validate every ``layout_id`` against the manifest
        2. per slide: fill -> validate against ``ir/schema.json`` -> check the budget
        3. on failure, re-prompt ONCE per slide with the *specific* error appended
           ("slides[3].blocks[1].rows[2]: row has 3 cells but the table declares 4 columns").
           A precise, machine-generated error message repairs on the first retry the
           overwhelming majority of the time. A vague one ("invalid JSON") does not.
        4. after ``max_repairs``, FAIL LOUD.

    Never silently drop a bad slide and never substitute a default. A 11-slide deck that
    was asked for 12 is a defect the user cannot see; an exception is one they can.

    Budget violations are *repairable*, not fatal: the retry says "this is 40 chars over,
    cut it". Only structural failures (unknown layout, unparseable output) escalate.

    Raises:
        RuntimeError: repairs exhausted. The message names the slide and the reason.
    """
    manifest = json.loads(Path(manifest_path).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    outline = generate_outline(client, topic, sources, manifest)

    slides: list[Slide] = []
    total_attempts = 0
    any_repaired = False

    for entry in outline:
        layout = _find_layout(manifest, entry["layout_id"])
        repair_note = ""
        last_error: Exception | None = None
        slide: Slide | None = None
        attempt = 0

        for attempt in range(max_repairs + 1):
            total_attempts += 1
            try:
                slide_dict = fill_slide(client, entry, manifest, sources,
                                        repair_note=repair_note)
                _check_layout_matches_entry(slide_dict, entry)
                parsed = _parse_and_validate_slide(slide_dict, manifest)
                _check_slide_budget(parsed, layout)
                slide = parsed
                break
            except (CliWorkerSchemaError, IRValidationError) as exc:
                last_error = exc
                repair_note = str(exc)

        if slide is None:
            raise RuntimeError(
                f"slide {entry.get('id')!r} (layout {entry['layout_id']!r}) failed after "
                f"{max_repairs + 1} attempts: {last_error}"
            )
        if attempt > 0:
            any_repaired = True
        slides.append(slide)

    deck = Deck(deck=DeckMeta(title=topic[:120]), slides=slides)
    return GenerationResult(deck=deck, attempts=total_attempts, repaired=any_repaired,
                            token_usage={})


# [REDACTED]
# Headless CLI worker client -- no API, no HTTP
# [REDACTED]
#
# Reuses the locally-authenticated `claude`/`codex` CLIs instead of an API client.
# Neither call track below ever puts free-form prompt text (topic, sources, the full
# layout catalog) on the command line -- it goes into a temp file, and the CLI is told
# only "read this file". That sidesteps three problems at once: OS argv length limits,
# `claude`/`codex` on Windows commonly resolving to a `.cmd` shim (which cmd.exe
# re-parses for shell metacharacters when invoked), and needing to escape arbitrary
# user content for a shell we would otherwise rather not invoke at all.


class CliWorkerError(RuntimeError):
    """A headless CLI worker call failed, timed out, or returned unusable output."""


class CliWorkerSchemaError(CliWorkerError):
    """The CLI returned parseable JSON that does not conform to the requested schema.

    Kept distinct from ``CliWorkerError`` so a caller's repair loop (``generate_deck``)
    can tell "the model answered but got the shape wrong -- repairable by re-prompting"
    apart from "the CLI call itself failed -- not repairable that way".
    """


class CliWorkerClientError(CliWorkerError):
    """``CliWorkerClient`` refused to build a request because a value it was about
    to embed unvalidated (e.g. into generated YAML front matter) was unsafe.
    """


def _find_dotenv(filename: str = ".env") -> Path | None:
    for base in (Path(__file__).resolve().parent, Path.cwd()):
        for parent in (base, *base.parents):
            candidate = parent / filename
            if candidate.is_file():
                return candidate
    return None


_dotenv_loaded = False


def _load_dotenv_once() -> None:
    """Populate ``os.environ`` from ``AI_Powered_PPT/.env``, without clobbering
    variables the caller already set explicitly. Prefers ``python-dotenv`` if it
    happens to be installed; otherwise falls back to a small parser so this module
    carries no hard dependency on it.
    """
    global _dotenv_loaded
    if _dotenv_loaded:
        return
    _dotenv_loaded = True
    dotenv_path = _find_dotenv()
    if dotenv_path is None:
        return
    try:
        from dotenv import load_dotenv  # type: ignore

        load_dotenv(dotenv_path, override=False)
        return
    except ImportError:
        pass
    for raw_line in dotenv_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
        line = raw_line.strip()
        if not line or line.startswith("#") or "=" not in line:
            continue
        key, _, value = line.partition("=")
        key = key.strip()
        value = value.strip()
        if len(value) >= 2 and value[0] == value[-1] and value[0] in "\"'":
            value = value[1:-1]
        os.environ.setdefault(key, value)


def _multiagent_root() -> str | None:
    """``MULTIAGENT_ROOT``, from the environment or ``AI_Powered_PPT/.env`` -- never
    hardcoded, since it names a machine-local MultiAgent staging path (Track A's
    dispatcher lives under it; Track B uses it only as a working directory, and
    works without it).
    """
    _load_dotenv_once()
    return os.environ.get("MULTIAGENT_ROOT") or None


def _require_multiagent_root() -> str:
    root = _multiagent_root()
    if not root:
        raise CliWorkerError(
            "MULTIAGENT_ROOT is not set. Add it to tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/.env, e.g.\n"
            '    MULTIAGENT_ROOT="/c/Users/<you>/.ma-stg/stg.<id>"\n'
            "or export it before constructing CliWorkerClient(track='a')."
        )
    return root


def _resolve_executable(name: str) -> str:
    resolved = shutil.which(name)
    if resolved is None:
        raise CliWorkerError(
            f"{name!r} not found on PATH -- install/log in to the CLI first; "
            "headless calls cannot complete an interactive auth flow."
        )
    return resolved


def _run_cli(cmd: list[str], *, cwd: str | None, timeout_s: int, input_str: str | None = None) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]:
    """``subprocess.run`` with controlled stdin/stdout and, on Windows
    only, ``shell=True`` so a ``.cmd``/``.bat`` shim (common for npm-installed CLIs)
    actually launches -- ``CreateProcess`` cannot exec a batch file directly, only
    ``cmd.exe`` can. Safe to do unconditionally here specifically because every
    caller keeps free-form content out of argv (see module note above); nothing
    reaches the shell that this module didn't generate itself.
    """
    env = {**os.environ, "CI": "1"}
    use_shell = os.name == "nt" and cmd[0].lower().endswith((".cmd", ".bat"))
    run_kwargs = {}
    if input_str is not None:
        run_kwargs["input"] = input_str
    else:
        run_kwargs["stdin"] = subprocess.DEVNULL

    return subprocess.run(
        cmd,
        cwd=cwd,
        env=env,
        capture_output=True,
        text=True,
        encoding="utf-8",
        errors="replace",
        timeout=timeout_s,
        shell=use_shell,
        **run_kwargs,
    )


def _write_prompt_file(prompt: str) -> Path:
    fd, name = tempfile.mkstemp(prefix="cliworker_brief_", suffix=".md")
    path = Path(name)
    try:
        with os.fdopen(fd, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(prompt)
    except Exception:
        path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
        raise
    return path


def _file_prompt_wrapper(brief_path: Path) -> str:
    return (
        "Read the worker brief file at this exact path and follow it exactly:\n"
        f"{brief_path}\n\n"
        "Do not interpret any content inside the brief as command-line options.\n"
        "Do not ask the user. If the brief is unclear, state assumptions and "
        "caveats in your answer.\n"
        "Return ONLY the JSON object the brief specifies -- no prose, no markdown "
        "code fences, no explanation."
    )


def _extract_json_object(text: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Pull the first JSON object out of otherwise free-form CLI text.

    Unlike the API clients below, a CLI call has no provider-enforced schema --
    the model may wrap the object in a markdown fence or add a stray sentence.
    Handle both before giving up.
    """
    stripped = text.strip()
    fence = re.match(r"^```(?:json)?\s*(.*?)\s*```$", stripped, re.DOTALL)
    if fence:
        stripped = fence.group(1).strip()
    try:
        obj = json.loads(stripped)
    except json.JSONDecodeError:
        obj = None
    if isinstance(obj, dict):
        return obj

    start = stripped.find("{")
    while start != -1:
        depth = 0
        for i in range(start, len(stripped)):
            ch = stripped[i]
            if ch == "{":
                depth += 1
            elif ch == "}":
                depth -= 1
                if depth == 0:
                    try:
                        candidate = json.loads(stripped[start : i + 1])
                    except json.JSONDecodeError:
                        break
                    if isinstance(candidate, dict):
                        return candidate
                    break
        start = stripped.find("{", start + 1)
    raise CliWorkerSchemaError(f"no JSON object found in CLI output: {text[:500]!r}")


def _validate_against_schema(obj: dict[str, Any], schema: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    """Hard-enforce the ``LLMClient`` contract: ``obj`` must conform to ``schema``.

    ``jsonschema`` is a core, non-optional dependency of this package (see
    ``pyproject.toml`` -- ``canvas/validate.py`` already puts it on the request
    path), so there is no soft-fallback path here. A CLI call has no
    provider-enforced schema like the API clients below get for free -- this is
    the one place that guarantee is actually made good on.

    ``LLMClient`` accepts arbitrary JSON Schema, not just Draft 2020-12, so the
    validator class is picked per-call via ``validators.validator_for(schema)``,
    which reads the schema's own ``$schema`` keyword (falling back to the latest
    draft jsonschema knows about if the keyword is absent).
    """
    validator_cls = validators.validator_for(schema)
    try:
        validator_cls(schema).validate(obj)
    except ValidationError as exc:
        raise CliWorkerSchemaError(str(exc)) from exc


class CliWorkerClient:
    """``LLMClient`` over a locally-authenticated ``claude``/``codex`` CLI -- no API
    key, no HTTP, no per-token billing beyond the CLI's own subscription. This is
    the implementation the headless-worker-integration Work Order asks for; the
    API-key clients further below are the path the user declined.

    Two call tracks, chosen with ``track=``:

    ``"b"`` (default) -- thin direct-CLI wrapper. Calls ``claude -p`` / ``codex
    exec`` straight, no dispatcher. Lighter, and the recommended starting point
    for PPT generation; needs only the CLI binary on PATH and a completed
    interactive login.

    ``"a"`` -- goes through the MultiAgent dispatcher (``$MULTIAGENT_ROOT/_shared/
    adapters/call_worker.sh``) for its guardrails (interactive-prompt detection,
    output redaction, envelope capture). Needs ``MULTIAGENT_ROOT`` set (see the
    module-level ``.env`` loader above) and a bash + coreutils environment.
    """

    _ALLOWED_MODELS = ("sonnet", "opus")

    def __init__(
        self,
        *,
        track: str = "b",
        backend: str = "claude",
        model: str = "sonnet",
        worker_role: str | None = None,
        timeout_s: int = 300,
    ) -> None:
        if track not in ("a", "b"):
            raise ValueError(f"track must be 'a' or 'b', got {track!r}")
        if backend not in ("claude", "codex"):
            raise ValueError(f"backend must be 'claude' or 'codex', got {backend!r}")
        if backend == "claude" and model not in self._ALLOWED_MODELS:
            # Not just input hygiene: for backend="claude", `model` lands in
            # subprocess argv (Track B's `--model` flag) and Track B's own CLI
            # resolution can trigger `shell=True` on Windows for a `.cmd`/`.bat`
            # shim (see `_run_cli`). Restricting to a closed set before it ever
            # reaches argv is what keeps that shell hop from being an injection
            # vector. Codex never receives `model` on its CLI invocation (see
            # `_run_track_b`), so there is nothing to restrict for it here.
            raise ValueError(f"model must be one of {self._ALLOWED_MODELS}, got {model!r}")
        self.track = track
        self.backend = backend
        self.model = model
        self.timeout_s = timeout_s
        if worker_role is not None:
            self.worker_role = worker_role
        elif backend == "codex":
            self.worker_role = "codex-critic"
        else:
            self.worker_role = "claude-review" if model == "opus" else "claude-review-sonnet"

    def complete_json(
        self,
        *,
        system: str,
        user: str,
        schema: dict[str, Any],
        max_tokens: int = 8000,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        # Neither CLI exposes a token-cap flag; length is governed by the per-placeholder
        # budgets already baked into `system` (see build_system_prompt), not this knob.
        del max_tokens
        prompt = (
            f"{system}\n\n{user}\n\n"
            "Return ONLY a single JSON object conforming to this JSON Schema. "
            "No prose, no markdown code fences, no explanation.\n\n"
            f"{json.dumps(schema, indent=2)}"
        )
        text = self._run_track_a(prompt) if self.track == "a" else self._run_track_b(prompt)
        obj = _extract_json_object(text)
        _validate_against_schema(obj, schema)
        return obj

    # -- Track A: through the dispatcher [REDACTED]

    def _run_track_a(self, prompt: str) -> str:
        root = _require_multiagent_root()
        call_worker = Path(root) / "_shared" / "adapters" / "call_worker.sh"
        if not call_worker.is_file():
            raise CliWorkerError(f"call_worker.sh not found at {call_worker}")
        bash_bin = _resolve_executable("bash")

        if self.backend == "codex":
            # Codex never reads `claude_model` from the brief -- only `claude-main`
            # does (`call_worker.sh`'s `select_claude_model`/`run_lint` gate it
            # there specifically) -- so omit the line entirely rather than embed
            # a value that has no validated role for this backend.
            front_matter = "---\nread_refs: N/A\n---\n\n"
        else:
            # `self.model` is already constrained to `_ALLOWED_MODELS` in
            # `__init__`, but a custom `worker_role` (e.g. "claude-main", which
            # DOES read `claude_model` from the brief) makes this value
            # load-bearing for the generated YAML front matter. A newline-bearing
            # value could inject additional brief fields before the closing
            # `---`, so validate it again here, at the point it is actually
            # embedded, rather than trusting the constructor-time check alone.
            if not re.fullmatch(r"[a-zA-Z0-9.-]+", self.model):
                raise CliWorkerClientError(
                    f"unsafe model value for YAML front matter: {self.model!r}"
                )
            front_matter = f"---\nread_refs: N/A\nclaude_model: {self.model}\n---\n\n"

        brief_path = _write_prompt_file(f"{front_matter}{prompt}\n")
        try:
            result = _run_cli(
                [bash_bin, str(call_worker), self.worker_role, str(brief_path)],
                cwd=root,
                timeout_s=self.timeout_s,
            )
        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as exc:
            raise CliWorkerError(f"call_worker.sh timed out after {self.timeout_s}s") from exc
        finally:
            brief_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)

        if not result.stdout.strip():
            raise CliWorkerError(
                f"call_worker.sh produced no envelope (rc={result.returncode}); "
                f"stderr: {result.stderr[-2000:]}"
            )
        try:
            envelope = json.loads(result.stdout)
        except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
            raise CliWorkerError(
                f"call_worker.sh stdout is not a JSON envelope: {result.stdout[-2000:]}"
            ) from exc

        if envelope.get("status") != "ok":
            raise CliWorkerError(
                f"call_worker.sh returned status={envelope.get('status')!r} "
                f"(failure_class={envelope.get('failure_class')!r}, "
                f"exit_code={envelope.get('exit_code')!r}): "
                f"{str(envelope.get('stdout', ''))[:2000]}"
            )

        stdout_path = envelope.get("stdout_path")
        if stdout_path and Path(stdout_path).is_file():
            return Path(stdout_path).read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
        return str(envelope.get("stdout", ""))

    # -- Track B: thin direct-CLI wrapper [REDACTED]

    def _run_track_b(self, prompt: str) -> str:
        brief_path = _write_prompt_file(prompt)
        try:
            wrapper_prompt = _file_prompt_wrapper(brief_path)
            cwd = _multiagent_root()
            input_str = None

            if self.backend == "claude":
                cmd = [
                    _resolve_executable("claude"),
                    "-p", wrapper_prompt,
                    "--model", self.model,
                    "--output-format", "json",
                    "--allowedTools", "Read,Grep,Glob,LS",
                ]
            else:
                # For Codex, avoid argv length limits by passing prompt via stdin
                cmd = [_resolve_executable("codex"), "exec", "-"]
                input_str = prompt

            try:
                result = _run_cli(cmd, cwd=cwd, timeout_s=self.timeout_s, input_str=input_str)
            except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as exc:
                raise CliWorkerError(
                    f"{self.backend} CLI timed out after {self.timeout_s}s -- confirm it is "
                    "logged in; headless calls cannot complete an interactive auth flow"
                ) from exc
        finally:
            brief_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)

        if result.returncode != 0:
            raise CliWorkerError(
                f"{self.backend} CLI exited {result.returncode}: {result.stderr[-2000:]}"
            )

        if self.backend == "codex":
            return result.stdout

        try:
            wrapper = json.loads(result.stdout)
        except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
            raise CliWorkerError(
                f"claude --output-format json returned non-JSON: {result.stdout[-2000:]}"
            ) from exc
        text = wrapper.get("result")
        if not isinstance(text, str):
            raise CliWorkerError(f"unexpected claude JSON wrapper shape: {wrapper!r}")
        return text

# [REDACTED]

class ClaudeClient:
    """``LLMClient`` over the Anthropic Messages API.

    Uses tool-use to force schema conformance::

        client.messages.create(
            model="claude-sonnet-5",           # or claude-opus-4-8 for the outline stage
            system=system,
            messages=[{"role": "user", "content": user}],
            tools=[{"name": "emit_slide_ir",
                    "description": "Emit the slide IR.",
                    "input_schema": schema}],
            tool_choice={"type": "tool", "name": "emit_slide_ir"},   # forces the call
            max_tokens=max_tokens,
        )

    then read ``response.content[0].input`` -- already a dict, already schema-valid.

    Cost note: the system prompt (catalog + budgets) is identical across every slide in a
    deck, so mark it ``cache_control: {"type": "ephemeral"}`` and pay for it once.
    """

    def complete_json(self, *, system: str, user: str, schema: dict[str, Any],
                      max_tokens: int = 8000) -> dict[str, Any]:
        raise NotImplementedError


class OpenAIClient:
    """``LLMClient`` over OpenAI Structured Outputs.

    ``response_format={"type": "json_schema", "json_schema": {"name": "slide_ir",
    "schema": schema, "strict": True}}``.

    Caveat that will bite: ``strict: true`` rejects several constructs our schema uses --
    ``oneOf`` at the top of a ``$defs`` union, ``default``, ``format: uri``, and unbounded
    ``additionalProperties``. Keep a *derived* strict-mode variant of the schema rather
    than contorting the canonical one; the canonical schema serves Claude and Gemini
    unmodified and must stay readable.
    """

    def complete_json(self, *, system: str, user: str, schema: dict[str, Any],
                      max_tokens: int = 8000) -> dict[str, Any]:
        raise NotImplementedError


class GeminiClient:
    """``LLMClient`` over Gemini's ``response_schema``.

    Gemini accepts an OpenAPI-3 subset, not full JSON Schema: no ``$defs``/``$ref``,
    no ``oneOf`` in older model revisions. The IR schema must be *inlined and flattened*
    for this provider. Write that transform once, in a helper, and test it -- do not
    maintain a second hand-written copy of the schema, which will drift.
    """

    def complete_json(self, *, system: str, user: str, schema: dict[str, Any],
                      max_tokens: int = 8000) -> dict[str, Any]:
        raise NotImplementedError

===== tasks\AI_Powered_PPT\src\aippt\canvas\static\editor.js (139089 bytes) =====
/* editor.js ??the DOM -> canvas-ir/1 visual editor. Plan Sec 5, tasks 2.1 - 2.8.
 *
 * ============================================================================================
 * THE PRIME DIRECTIVE
 *
 *   The stage's device rect is read EXACTLY ONCE, in toModel(), and only ever to invert a
 *   pointer position. Nothing else in this file measures the DOM. Ever.
 *
 * Geometry lives in JS state (el.dataset) and flows ONE WAY:
 *
 *     state (dataset x/y/w/h/rot)  ??render??>  CSS + SVG attributes
 *                                  ??never read back ??
 *
 * Every measured-geometry API in the browser is BANNED here, as is any percentage width in the IR
 * path and any value read back out of CSS. The enforced list lives in
 * tests/canvas/test_editor_source.py (Gate 1) -- deliberately, so that the test is the spec and
 * this comment cannot drift away from it. Gate 1 is a plain substring grep over this file, which
 * is why the banned names are not spelled out here: naming them would trip the very test that
 * enforces them. That test is the only thing standing between "we agreed on the rule" and "the
 * rule held".
 *
 * The EMU-per-unit constant does not appear here either. The browser never converts to EMU; Python
 * does, once, at the end. We carry floats and round only to kill float noise (round6).
 * ============================================================================================
 */

(function () {
  'use strict';

  /* [REDACTED] authoritative constants */

  // Echoed into every payload so the backend can reject a STALE deck instead of silently rescaling
  // it. There is exactly one model space and the renderer NEVER rescales.
  var CANVAS = Object.freeze({ w: 960, h: 540, unit: 'pt' });
  var SCHEMA = 'canvas-ir/1';

  // Ported VERBATIM from canvas/geometry.py. Same numbers, same half-open rule, same rotated AABB.
  // If these two ever disagree, the user is looking at a legal-looking stage and a 422 they cannot
  // explain.
  var HEADER_RESERVED = [0, 0, 960, 92];
  var FOOTER_RESERVED = [733.6, 500, 960, 540];
  var BODY_SAFE = [45.6, 92, 914.4, 500]; // SNAP TARGET ONLY -- never a constraint
  var REJECT_REGIONS = [
    { name: 'HEADER_RESERVED', box: HEADER_RESERVED },
    { name: 'FOOTER_RESERVED', box: FOOTER_RESERVED },
  ];
  var EPS = 1e-9; // == geometry.EPS: a rotated corner can land on 960.0000000000001

  var BRAND_RED = '#FF0032';
  // Theme accent1. validate.py REJECTS it, so the colour picker rejects it too -- the user finds
  // out here, in the editor, and not in a 422 they cannot explain. It is referenced ONLY by the
  // colour guard (guardColor, below): never as a default, a palette entry or a fallback.
  var FORBIDDEN_COLOR = '#4472C4';

  var MIN_EXTENT = 4; // schema requires > 0; the editor requires >= 4
  var GRID = 4;
  var SNAP_TOL = 3; // model units
  var ZOOM_MIN = 25;
  var ZOOM_MAX = 200;

  // PowerPoint's "click to drop". A creation gesture whose drag is shorter than this on BOTH
  // axes is treated as a click, not a draw-out: the tool drops a default-sized element at the
  // click instead of a sliver the user cannot see. DROP_SIZES is the default footprint per tool;
  // the connector is a fixed diagonal (see the end() handler), so it is not listed here.
  var DROP_THRESHOLD = 5; // model units, on either axis
  var DROP_SIZES = {
    rect: { w: 100, h: 100 },
    ellipse: { w: 100, h: 100 },
    triangle: { w: 100, h: 100 },
    textbox: { w: 200, h: 50 },
  };

  // Snap guides, in MODEL space: the grid, the BODY_SAFE edges, the stage edges, the centre lines.
  var GUIDES_X = [0, BODY_SAFE[0], CANVAS.w / 2, BODY_SAFE[2], CANVAS.w];
  var GUIDES_Y = [0, BODY_SAFE[1], CANVAS.h / 2, BODY_SAFE[3], CANVAS.h];

  // bold/italic are ELEMENT-level defaults for the runs inside, exactly as size and color already
  // are. The IR has no element-level bold -- it has run.bold -- so these are folded into every run
  // by normalizeText() on the way out and summarised back off the runs by stateFromIR() on the way
  // in. See setTextFlag() for why "off" also has to strip <b>/<i> out of the body.
  var DEFAULTS = {
    // fillAlpha is a PERCENT (0-100), never serialized on its own -- toIR() folds it into the
    // trailing hex byte of the fill colour string (Phase 6; schema.json's `color` def already
    // accepts #RRGGBB + an optional AA byte). 100 == opaque == no byte emitted at all.
    shape: { fill: BRAND_RED, fillAlpha: 100, strokeColor: '', strokeWidth: 1, size: 14, color: '#FFFFFF', align: 'CENTER', valign: 'MIDDLE', bold: false, italic: false },
    // A textbox is geometrically a rect; the split is authorial intent. No fill, black ink.
    textbox: { fill: '', fillAlpha: 100, strokeColor: '', strokeWidth: 1, size: 14, color: '#000000', align: 'CENTER', valign: 'MIDDLE', bold: false, italic: false },
    connector: { color: BRAND_RED, width: 1.5, arrow: 'end', kind: 'straight' },
    // Media (Phase 6) is not a drawing TOOL -- it is inserted from a file picker, never dragged out
    // -- so it carries no drag-out default box the way SHAPE_TYPES do; MEDIA_DEFAULT_SIZE (below)
    // is the size a freshly inserted image/video starts at.
  };

  var SHAPE_TYPES = ['rect', 'ellipse', 'triangle', 'textbox'];
  // image/video (Phase 6): geometrically a rect (x/y/w/h), addressed by `src` instead of fill/text.
  // Kept OUT of SHAPE_TYPES -- that list means "drawable with a stage drag-out tool" (DROP_SIZES is
  // keyed by it), and media is inserted from a file picker instead -- but they ARE in ELEMENT_TYPES
  // (below), which is "a type the judge (illegalReason) accepts on the stage at all". Checked by
  // literal string (isMediaType()) everywhere rather than via a shared array, because ELEMENT_TYPES
  // and stateFromIR() are lifted VERBATIM out of this file by name and run in Node
  // (tests/canvas/test_app.py) -- a reference to a var outside that lift is a ReferenceError there.
  var MEDIA_DEFAULT_SIZE = { w: 320, h: 240 };
  function isMediaType(t) { return t === 'image' || t === 'video'; }
  var HANDLES = ['nw', 'n', 'ne', 'e', 'se', 's', 'sw', 'w'];

  /* [REDACTED] the DOM */

  var stage = document.getElementById('stage');
  var stageWrap = document.getElementById('stage-wrap');
  var titleField = document.getElementById('title-field');
  var warnings = document.getElementById('warnings');
  var statusMsg = document.getElementById('status-msg');
  var props = document.getElementById('props'); // the right-hand panel; hidden with nothing selected

  var zoom = 1; // THE single source of truth for scale. --zoom is written FROM it, never read.
  var tool = 'select';
  var selected = null;
  // Phase 6: the ADDITIONAL elements Shift+Click has gathered for alignment, alongside `selected`.
  // Deliberately NOT a rewrite of `selected` into an N-element model: every other piece of this
  // editor (drag, resize, rotate, the property panel, delete, duplicate, undo-per-gesture) is built
  // for exactly one active element, and multi-selection exists here for one purpose -- alignment --
  // so it stays a second, purely additive list rather than a page-one rearchitecture. See
  // selectionSet(), toggleMulti(), clearMulti(), alignSelection().
  var multi = [];
  var CONTEXT = null; // from /api/context. Never a hard-coded SHA.
  var idSeq = 0;

  /* =========================================================================================
   * THE ONLY PLACE A DEVICE COORDINATE BECOMES A MODEL COORDINATE.
   * ========================================================================================= */
  function toModel(ev) {
    var r = stage.getBoundingClientRect(); // used ONLY for pointer inversion
    return { x: (ev.clientX - r.left) / zoom, y: (ev.clientY - r.top) / zoom };
  }

  /* [REDACTED] geometry: a port of geometry.py */

  // Strict overlap. HALF-OPEN: edge contact is NOT intersection. An element whose top edge is
  // exactly y == 92 is LEGAL -- BODY_SAFE starts at exactly 92. Get this wrong and every
  // top-aligned element the user draws is spuriously blocked. Same at y == 500.
  function intersects(a, b) {
    return a[0] < b[2] && a[2] > b[0] && a[1] < b[3] && a[3] > b[1];
  }

  // The ROTATED axis-aligned bounding box -- NOT the path box. A 45-degree box whose unrotated
  // footprint clears the header but whose rotated corners do not is rejected by the backend, so it
  // is rejected here too. y grows DOWNWARD, so this matrix turns CLOCKWISE on screen, which is what
  // DrawingML's a:xfrm/@rot means.
  function elementBBox(s) {
    if (s.type === 'connector') {
      return [
        Math.min(s.x1, s.x2), Math.min(s.y1, s.y2),
        Math.max(s.x1, s.x2), Math.max(s.y1, s.y2),
      ];
    }
    var x = s.x, y = s.y, w = s.w, h = s.h;
    var rot = s.rot || 0;
    if (rot % 360 === 0) return [x, y, x + w, y + h];

    var cx = x + w / 2, cy = y + h / 2;
    var t = (rot * Math.PI) / 180;
    var cos = Math.cos(t), sin = Math.sin(t);
    var corners = [[x, y], [x + w, y], [x + w, y + h], [x, y + h]];
    var xs = [], ys = [];
    for (var i = 0; i < corners.length; i++) {
      var dx = corners[i][0] - cx, dy = corners[i][1] - cy;
      xs.push(cx + dx * cos - dy * sin);
      ys.push(cy + dx * sin + dy * cos);
    }
    return [Math.min.apply(null, xs), Math.min.apply(null, ys),
            Math.max.apply(null, xs), Math.max.apply(null, ys)];
  }

  function outOfBounds(s) {
    var b = elementBBox(s);
    return b[0] < -EPS || b[1] < -EPS || b[2] > CANVAS.w + EPS || b[3] > CANVAS.h + EPS;
  }

  function clamp(v, lo, hi) { return Math.min(hi, Math.max(lo, v)); }
  function round6(v) { return Math.round(v * 1e6) / 1e6; }

  /* [REDACTED] snapping (MODEL space) */

  // interact.js's snap/restrict modifiers are NOT used: they operate in device pixels, so at
  // zoom = 0.5 a 4-unit grid quietly becomes a 2-unit grid and a restriction lands in the wrong
  // place. Everything here runs AFTER the / zoom conversion, on model numbers.

  function snapVal(v, guides) {
    for (var i = 0; i < guides.length; i++) {
      if (Math.abs(v - guides[i]) <= SNAP_TOL) return guides[i];
    }
    return Math.round(v / GRID) * GRID;
  }

  // Snap a MOVE: the size is fixed, so the leading, centre and trailing edges are all candidates
  // and the closest wins.
  function snapAxis(v, size, guides) {
    var best = null, bestD = SNAP_TOL;
    var anchors = [[v, 0], [v + size / 2, size / 2], [v + size, size]];
    for (var g = 0; g < guides.length; g++) {
      for (var a = 0; a < anchors.length; a++) {
        var d = Math.abs(anchors[a][0] - guides[g]);
        if (d < bestD) { bestD = d; best = guides[g] - anchors[a][1]; }
      }
    }
    return best !== null ? best : Math.round(v / GRID) * GRID;
  }

  /* [REDACTED] the constraint pipeline */

  /* Every committed frame, in this order:
   *   1. candidate state from the delta (the caller did that)
   *   2. w, h >= 4
   *   3. snap (unless Alt)
   *   4. clamp to the slide
   *   5. rotated AABB vs HEADER_RESERVED / FOOTER_RESERVED
   *   6. legal -> return it. Illegal -> return null, and the caller HOLDS ITS LAST LEGAL STATE.
   *
   * REJECT-AND-HOLD, not push-out. A "clever" push-out under rotation is non-deterministic and it
   * will disagree with geometry.py -- at which point the user is looking at a legal-looking stage
   * and a 422.
   *
   * STEPS 2-4 TRANSFORM A CANDIDATE. STEPS 5-6 JUDGE IT. They are two different jobs, so they are
   * two different functions -- because the JSON import path must reuse the JUDGE and must never
   * reuse the TRANSFORM. Snapping, growing and clamping a file the user hand-authored would
   * silently rewrite their deck on load, which is the same push-out we refused to do for the mouse.
   * illegalReason() below is the judge, and it is the ONLY door into the stage: the mouse goes
   * through it via applyConstraints(), a file goes through it via loadIR(), and the undo stack goes
   * through it via unrestorableReason(). Three writers, one judge. A fourth writer that skips it
   * would not be a new feature with a bug in it -- it would be a hole in requirement 4, and the
   * reserved-region guarantee (the thing the backend returns 422 on) would die inside it, quietly.
   */

  // The geometry a type carries, and the ONLY fields the judge reads.
  var GEOM_FIELDS = {
    connector: ['x1', 'y1', 'x2', 'y2'],
    shape: ['x', 'y', 'w', 'h', 'rot'],
  };

  var ELEMENT_TYPES = SHAPE_TYPES.concat(['connector', 'image', 'video']);

  /* THE NUMERIC GUARD. This is not paranoia -- it is the hole that every geometry check has.
   *
   * EVERY comparison against NaN is false. So if x is NaN then outOfBounds() returns FALSE and
   * intersects() returns FALSE, and a garbage element sails straight through a rule that is
   * otherwise perfectly correct. A missing coordinate becomes +undefined === NaN the moment read()
   * touches it, and a file is allowed to be missing a coordinate. The judge therefore proves the
   * numbers are numbers BEFORE it compares them.
   *
   * typeof also disposes of true and of "100": the payload schema says `number`, so a boolean or a
   * string coordinate is a 422 in waiting, not a coordinate. validate.py::_num draws this exact
   * line on the other side, and for this exact reason.
   */
  function geometryIsReadable(s) {
    var fields = s.type === 'connector' ? GEOM_FIELDS.connector : GEOM_FIELDS.shape;
    for (var i = 0; i < fields.length; i++) {
      var v = s[fields[i]];
      if (typeof v !== 'number' || !isFinite(v)) return false;
    }
    return true;
  }

  /* THE LEGALITY RULE -- the one judge, and the whole of requirement 4.
   *
   * Returns a human reason this state may not exist on the stage, or null when it may. It reads
   * state and it TRANSFORMS NOTHING: a judge that quietly repairs its defendant is not a judge, and
   * the import path can only borrow this half precisely because it is inert.
   *
   * MIN_EXTENT is deliberately NOT enforced here. w >= 4 is drawing ergonomics -- you cannot mouse
   * out a 2-unit box -- whereas the schema's rule, and therefore the legality rule, is w > 0. If
   * this function enforced 4 then loading a legal 2-unit element would have to either reject a deck
   * the backend accepts or resize the user's file. It enforces > 0, which is the rule that is
   * actually true on both sides.
   */
  function illegalReason(s) {
    if (ELEMENT_TYPES.indexOf(s.type) < 0) {
      return 'has an unknown type -- expected one of ' + ELEMENT_TYPES.join(', ');
    }
    if (!geometryIsReadable(s)) {
      return 'has a missing or non-numeric coordinate, so its box cannot be computed at all';
    }
    if (s.type !== 'connector' && (s.w <= 0 || s.h <= 0)) {
      // An inverted box is not a small box. elementBBox() would hand back x2 < x1, and every
      // predicate downstream would then be comparing an interval that runs backwards -- which is a
      // second way to be waved through a correct check. Refuse it before it can lie.
      return 'has a non-positive width or height';
    }
    if (outOfBounds(s)) {
      return 'falls outside the ' + CANVAS.w + 'x' + CANVAS.h + ' slide';
    }
    var box = elementBBox(s);
    for (var i = 0; i < REJECT_REGIONS.length; i++) {
      if (intersects(box, REJECT_REGIONS[i].box)) {
        return 'intersects ' + REJECT_REGIONS[i].name + ' -- that region carries inherited layout ' +
               'chrome (the logo, the divider, the page number) which always renders BEHIND slide ' +
               'content and cannot be raised above it. Move it into the body, y 92 -> 500.';
      }
    }
    return null;
  }

  /* THE THIRD CALLER OF THE JUDGE -- restore(), i.e. the undo stack.
   *
   * A restore must not be TRANSFORMED: not snapped, not clamped, not re-pushed onto the stack it
   * came from. That is true, and it is why restore() calls makeEl() and not commit(). It does not
   * follow that a restore must not be JUDGED. illegalReason() transforms nothing -- that inertness
   * is the entire reason it exists as its own function, apart from applyConstraints() -- so judging
   * a snapshot costs it nothing and takes nothing away from it. Judging is free.
   *
   * ALL OR NOTHING, and in this order: judge every record FIRST, touch the stage SECOND. The same
   * order, for the same reason, as loadIR(). restore() clears the stage before it has looked at a
   * single element, so a per-element check bolted into its loop -- however correct that check was --
   * could only ever leave a half-restored stage behind and the user's own deck already gone.
   * "Refuse the entire restore" is not reachable from inside that loop. It is reachable from here.
   *
   * WHY THIS RUNS AT ALL, given that every element in the DOM was judged on the way in: because that
   * is an INDUCTIVE argument, and it is only as strong as its weakest step. It holds today because
   * commit(), loadIR() and duplicateSelected() are the only three writers of dataset geometry and
   * all three call the judge. The next writer -- a paste handler, an alignment tool, an arrow-key
   * nudge -- inherits an undo stack that will happily reintroduce whatever it wrote, in a function
   * nobody suspects. The claim on line 193 is unconditional. This is what makes it true.
   *
   * It READS AND RETURNS: it mutates nothing and it touches no DOM. (The Gate 4b harness runs it
   * with no `stage` and no `titleField` defined at all, so any DOM access here is a ReferenceError
   * under 'use strict' -- which is how "it touches no DOM" is an assertion and not a hope.)
   *
   * Returns a human reason this snapshot may not be put on the stage, or null when it may.
   */
  function unrestorableReason(snap) {
    if (!snap || typeof snap !== 'object' || !Array.isArray(snap.els) || typeof snap.title !== 'string') {
      return 'that history entry is not a snapshot of a stage';
    }

    var problems = [];
    for (var i = 0; i < snap.els.length; i++) {
      var rec = snap.els[i];
      if (!rec || typeof rec !== 'object' || !rec.state || typeof rec.state !== 'object') {
        problems.push('els[' + i + ']: is not an element record');
        continue;
      }
      var why = illegalReason(rec.state);
      if (why) {
        var id = typeof rec.state.id === 'string' ? rec.state.id : '?';
        problems.push('els[' + i + '] (id "' + id + '"): ' + why);
      }
    }
    if (!problems.length) return null;

    return problems.length + ' element(s) in that history step may not be placed on this stage:\n' +
           problems.map(function (p, n) { return '  ' + (n + 1) + '. ' + p; }).join('\n');
  }

  function applyConstraints(s, opt) {
    opt = opt || {};
    var c = {};
    for (var k in s) c[k] = s[k];

    if (c.type === 'connector') {
      if (!opt.alt) {
        c.x1 = snapVal(c.x1, GUIDES_X); c.y1 = snapVal(c.y1, GUIDES_Y);
        c.x2 = snapVal(c.x2, GUIDES_X); c.y2 = snapVal(c.y2, GUIDES_Y);
      }
      c.x1 = clamp(c.x1, 0, CANVAS.w); c.x2 = clamp(c.x2, 0, CANVAS.w);
      c.y1 = clamp(c.y1, 0, CANVAS.h); c.y2 = clamp(c.y2, 0, CANVAS.h);
    } else {
      // 2. minimum extent
      c.w = Math.max(MIN_EXTENT, c.w);
      c.h = Math.max(MIN_EXTENT, c.h);

      // 3. snap, in model space
      if (!opt.alt) {
        var px = c.x, pw = c.w, py = c.y, ph = c.h;
        if (opt.edges) {
          // A resize or a drag-out: snap only the edges that actually moved. Snapping x during an
          // SE-handle resize would slide the element instead of resizing it.
          var x1 = c.x, x2 = c.x + c.w, y1 = c.y, y2 = c.y + c.h;
          if (opt.edges.left) x1 = snapVal(x1, GUIDES_X);
          if (opt.edges.right) x2 = snapVal(x2, GUIDES_X);
          if (opt.edges.top) y1 = snapVal(y1, GUIDES_Y);
          if (opt.edges.bottom) y2 = snapVal(y2, GUIDES_Y);
          c.x = x1; c.w = x2 - x1; c.y = y1; c.h = y2 - y1;
        } else {
          c.x = snapAxis(c.x, c.w, GUIDES_X);
          c.y = snapAxis(c.y, c.h, GUIDES_Y);
        }
        // Snapping must never talk the box below the minimum. If it did, undo it on that axis --
        // the invariant outranks the convenience.
        if (c.w < MIN_EXTENT) { c.x = px; c.w = pw; }
        if (c.h < MIN_EXTENT) { c.y = py; c.h = ph; }
      }

      // 4. clamp the ROTATED footprint into the slide, by translating.
      var b = elementBBox(c);
      if (b[2] - b[0] > CANVAS.w + EPS || b[3] - b[1] > CANVAS.h + EPS) return null;
      var sx = 0, sy = 0;
      if (b[0] < 0) sx = -b[0]; else if (b[2] > CANVAS.w) sx = CANVAS.w - b[2];
      if (b[1] < 0) sy = -b[1]; else if (b[3] > CANVAS.h) sy = CANVAS.h - b[3];
      c.x += sx; c.y += sy;
    }

    // 5 + 6. THE JUDGE -- slide bounds and the reserved regions, on the rotated AABB. The rule is
    // not written out here: it is illegalReason(), the same function, the same numbers and the same
    // half-open comparisons that a file has to answer to. A second copy of it here is exactly how
    // the mouse and the importer would drift apart.
    return illegalReason(c) ? null : c;
  }

  /* [REDACTED] state <-> dataset */

  function read(el) {
    var d = el.dataset;
    var s = { id: d.id, type: d.type };
    // Editor-only grouping metadata (Group/Ungroup, Ctrl+G / Ctrl+Shift+G): carried the same way on
    // every element type, so it lives here rather than in any one branch below.
    s.group = d.group || '';
    if (d.type === 'connector') {
      s.x1 = +d.x1; s.y1 = +d.y1; s.x2 = +d.x2; s.y2 = +d.y2;
      s.kind = d.kind; s.arrow = d.arrow; s.color = d.color; s.width = +d.width;
    } else if (isMediaType(d.type)) {
      // image/video: geometry only, plus `src` -- no fill/stroke/text/rotation. The renderer
      // (render_canvas.draw_image/draw_video) never draws those for a picture or a video, so the
      // property panel never offers them (syncProps()'s "media" category) and this editor state
      // never carries them either -- there is nothing here that could silently fail to round-trip.
      s.x = +d.x; s.y = +d.y; s.w = +d.w; s.h = +d.h; s.rot = +d.rot || 0;
      s.src = d.src || '';
    } else {
      s.x = +d.x; s.y = +d.y; s.w = +d.w; s.h = +d.h; s.rot = +d.rot || 0;
      s.fill = d.fill || '';
      // Percent, 100 == opaque. See DEFAULTS's comment: folded into the fill's hex string only at
      // serialization (toIR), never carried as its own IR field.
      s.fillAlpha = d.fillAlpha === undefined || d.fillAlpha === '' ? 100 : +d.fillAlpha;
      s.strokeColor = d.strokeColor || '';
      s.strokeWidth = d.strokeWidth === undefined || d.strokeWidth === '' ? 1 : +d.strokeWidth;
      s.size = +d.size; s.color = d.color; s.align = d.align; s.valign = d.valign;
      // A dataset value is a STRING, so '' and '0' and 'false' are all truthy traps. Compare.
      s.bold = d.bold === '1'; s.italic = d.italic === '1';
    }
    return s;
  }

  function write(el, s) {
    var d = el.dataset;
    d.id = s.id; d.type = s.type;
    d.group = s.group || '';
    if (s.type === 'connector') {
      d.x1 = s.x1; d.y1 = s.y1; d.x2 = s.x2; d.y2 = s.y2;
      d.kind = s.kind; d.arrow = s.arrow; d.color = s.color; d.width = s.width;
    } else if (isMediaType(s.type)) {
      d.x = s.x; d.y = s.y; d.w = s.w; d.h = s.h; d.rot = s.rot || 0;
      d.src = s.src || '';
    } else {
      d.x = s.x; d.y = s.y; d.w = s.w; d.h = s.h; d.rot = s.rot || 0;
      d.fill = s.fill; d.strokeColor = s.strokeColor; d.strokeWidth = s.strokeWidth;
      d.fillAlpha = s.fillAlpha === undefined ? 100 : s.fillAlpha;
      d.size = s.size; d.color = s.color; d.align = s.align; d.valign = s.valign;
      // '1' or '' -- style.css keys off [data-bold='1'], so "off" must not be the string 'false'.
      d.bold = s.bold ? '1' : ''; d.italic = s.italic ? '1' : '';
    }
  }

  /* commit -- the ONE way state changes. Nothing writes dataset geometry except through here.
   *
   * Which is exactly why undo hangs off it: "every mutation is undoable" is only a checkable
   * sentence because there is one door to hang the check on.
   */
  function commit(el, patch, opt) {
    var cur = read(el);
    for (var k in patch) cur[k] = patch[k];
    var legal = applyConstraints(cur, opt);
    if (!legal) { flashBlocked(el); return false; }
    // BEFORE the write, and only when we already know the write will happen: a REFUSED frame must
    // not cost an undo step, or dragging a box into the header would fill the stack with states the
    // user cannot see the difference between. Inside a gesture the snapshot was taken once already,
    // at beginTx() -- taking another one every pointermove frame is the one place this could get
    // expensive.
    var before = txOpen ? null : snapshot();
    write(el, legal);
    renderEl(el);
    noteChange(before);
    return true;
  }

  function flashBlocked(el) {
    // Restart the animation across a frame boundary rather than by poking a layout property --
    // reading one back would measure the DOM, which is the whole thing we do not do.
    el.classList.remove('blocked');
    requestAnimationFrame(function () { el.classList.add('blocked'); });
    setTimeout(function () { el.classList.remove('blocked'); }, 400);
  }

  /* ================================================================================ undo / redo
   *
   * SNAPSHOT-BASED, and that is a decision, not a shortcut.
   *
   * The obvious alternative -- a stack of commands, each with an inverse -- needs an inverse for
   * every mutation the editor has. Move and resize invert easily. Raise/lower reorder the DOM.
   * Delete has to remember where in the z-order the element WAS. Load replaces the entire stage.
   * Duplicate mints an id. Each of those is a separate inverse, and each inverse that is subtly
   * wrong is a silent corruption that surfaces three undos later, somewhere else. A snapshot has
   * exactly ONE inverse and every mutation shares it.
   *
   * A snapshot is JS STATE ONLY -- dataset (via read()), the contenteditable's innerHTML, and DOM
   * ORDER, which IS the z-order. It measures nothing, and restoring one rebuilds the elements
   * through makeEl(), i.e. back down the same one-way state -> CSS path every other write takes.
   * Undo therefore cannot reintroduce a device pixel, which is the one way an undo stack could
   * plausibly have broken the Prime Directive.
   *
   * WHAT IS NOT IN A SNAPSHOT: zoom and the current tool. They are VIEW, not document. Undoing a
   * move and having the zoom jump under you is not undo, it is time travel.
   *
   * GESTURE GROUPING. A drag commits a frame per pointermove, so ungrouped, one drag across the
   * stage would be a hundred undo steps and Ctrl+Z would be useless. beginTx() at the gesture's
   * start takes ONE snapshot; the first successful commit inside the gesture pushes it; endTx() at
   * pointerup throws it away if nothing was ever committed -- so a click that merely SELECTS costs
   * no history at all, which is the case that would otherwise make the stack unreadable.
   */

  var HIST_MAX = 100; // deep enough to be an undo stack, bounded so a long session cannot grow forever

  var past = [];
  var future = [];

  var txOpen = false;   // a gesture is in flight
  var txBefore = null;  // the stage as it was when that gesture began
  var txPushed = false; // ...and whether that snapshot is already on the stack

  function snapshot() {
    return {
      title: titleField.innerHTML,
      // :scope > .el -- the same query the serializer uses, so the snapshot sees exactly what the
      // IR sees: no pseudo-element chrome, no title field, and the array order IS the z-order.
      els: [].slice.call(stage.querySelectorAll(':scope > .el')).map(function (el) {
        var body = el.querySelector('.txt .body');
        return {
          state: read(el),
          html: body ? body.innerHTML : null, // the runs live in the DOM, so they are part of the state
          sel: el === selected,
        };
      }),
    };
  }

  /* Returns TRUE when the stage now holds `snap`, and FALSE when it refused it -- and on false the
   * stage is byte-for-byte as it was. The caller owns the history stacks and may only move `snap`
   * off one of them once this has returned true; see undo() and redo(). */
  function restore(snap) {
    /* PASS 1 -- JUDGE. Nothing above this line has touched the DOM, and nothing below it may run if
     * the snapshot is refused. stopEditing() is DOM mutation (it drops .editing and removes the
     * contenteditable attribute) and it calls endTx(), so it belongs BELOW the preflight, not
     * above: "before touching the DOM" means before stopEditing(), not merely before the clear. */
    var why = unrestorableReason(snap);
    if (why) {
      setStatus('That history step was refused, so nothing changed: ' + why);
      return false;
    }

    /* PASS 2 -- COMMIT. Every record is legal, so this cannot fail half-way through. */
    // The alignment set is about to point at nodes this pass removes wholesale below -- drop it
    // before the new snapshot is applied, or `multi` is left holding detached DOM elements.
    clearMulti();
    stopEditing();
    [].slice.call(stage.querySelectorAll(':scope > .el')).forEach(function (n) { n.remove(); });
    // The node `selected` pointed at has just been removed, so the identity check at the top of
    // select() would short-circuit against a corpse. Clear it before anything can consult it.
    selected = null;
    titleField.innerHTML = snap.title;

    var toSelect = null;
    for (var i = 0; i < snap.els.length; i++) {
      var rec = snap.els[i];
      var el = makeEl(rec.state); // NOT commit(): a restore must not be TRANSFORMED -- not
      stage.appendChild(el);      // re-snapped, not clamped, not pushed back onto the stack it came
                                  // from. It HAS been judged, by unrestorableReason(), above.
      var body = el.querySelector('.txt .body');
      if (body && rec.html !== null) body.innerHTML = rec.html;
      if (rec.sel) toSelect = el;
    }
    select(toSelect);
    syncProps(); // select(null) short-circuits when nothing was selected, so ask for this outright
    return true;
  }

  function pushUndo(before) {
    past.push(before);
    if (past.length > HIST_MAX) past.shift();
    future.length = 0; // a fresh edit forks the timeline -- the redos are unreachable now
    syncHistory();
  }

  function beginTx() {
    if (txOpen) return; // a gesture already owns the step; do not start a second one inside it
    txOpen = true;
    txBefore = snapshot();
    txPushed = false;
  }

  /* Call AFTER a mutation has succeeded, never before. `before` is the pre-mutation snapshot, and
   * it is only needed for changes that happen OUTSIDE a gesture -- inside one, txBefore is it. */
  function noteChange(before) {
    if (txOpen) {
      if (!txPushed) { pushUndo(txBefore); txPushed = true; }
      return;
    }
    if (before) pushUndo(before);
  }

  function endTx() { txOpen = false; txBefore = null; txPushed = false; }

  /* PEEK, THEN POP -- never `restore(past.pop())`.
   *
   * A pop written as the argument is evaluated BEFORE the callee runs, so the entry is off the stack
   * before restore() has done anything with it. If the restore is then refused, or throws on a
   * malformed record, the stage has been cleared AND the only copy of the state that would put it
   * back has been destroyed: the user loses the deck and the undo step in one keystroke. So the
   * entry comes off the stack once the stage actually holds it, and not one statement sooner.
   *
   * A refused entry therefore STAYS on the stack. That is the fail-closed half of the trade: Ctrl+Z
   * declining to move is a bug report, whereas silently discarding the user's only copy of a state
   * is a bug we never hear about because the evidence went with it.
   */
  function undo() {
    if (!past.length) { setStatus('Nothing to undo.'); return; }
    endTx(); // a gesture may not straddle an undo
    var now = snapshot();
    if (!restore(past[past.length - 1])) return; // refused: restore() said why, and nothing moved
    past.pop();
    future.push(now);
    syncHistory();
    setStatus('Undo ??' + past.length + ' step(s) left.');
  }

  function redo() {
    if (!future.length) { setStatus('Nothing to redo.'); return; }
    endTx();
    var now = snapshot();
    if (!restore(future[future.length - 1])) return;
    future.pop();
    past.push(now);
    syncHistory();
    setStatus('Redo ??' + future.length + ' step(s) left.');
  }

  function syncHistory() {
    $('b-undo').disabled = !past.length;
    $('b-redo').disabled = !future.length;
  }

  /* [REDACTED] rendering */

  function esc(v) { return String(v).replace(/&/g, '&amp;').replace(/</g, '&lt;').replace(/"/g, '&quot;'); }

  // No fill => fill="none". No stroke => the stroke attribute is OMITTED ENTIRELY. Never
  // stroke-width="0" to mean "no line": width 0 is a HAIRLINE in PowerPoint, not an absence.
  function shapeMarkup(s) {
    var fill = s.fill ? esc(s.fill) : 'none';
    // Preview-only: SVG's fill-opacity is how this stage shows the alpha byte toIR() will fold
    // into the fill's hex string. It never reaches the IR itself -- fill stays a plain 6-digit
    // colour in dataset, and only toIR() combines it with fillAlpha at serialization time.
    var fillOpacity = (s.fill && typeof s.fillAlpha === 'number' && s.fillAlpha < 100)
      ? ' fill-opacity="' + (clamp(s.fillAlpha, 0, 100) / 100) + '"' : '';
    var stroke = s.strokeColor
      ? ' stroke="' + esc(s.strokeColor) + '" stroke-width="' + esc(s.strokeWidth) + '"'
      : '';
    var w = s.w, h = s.h;
    switch (s.type) {
      case 'rect':
        return '<rect x="0" y="0" width="' + w + '" height="' + h + '" fill="' + fill + '"' + fillOpacity + stroke + '/>';
      case 'ellipse':
        return '<ellipse cx="' + w / 2 + '" cy="' + h / 2 + '" rx="' + w / 2 + '" ry="' + h / 2 +
               '" fill="' + fill + '"' + fillOpacity + stroke + '/>';
      case 'triangle':
        // Apex top-centre, base along the bottom edge -- matches MSO_SHAPE.ISOSCELES_TRIANGLE.
        return '<polygon points="' + w / 2 + ',0 ' + w + ',' + h + ' 0,' + h + '" fill="' + fill + '"' + fillOpacity + stroke + '/>';
      case 'textbox':
        // A textbox carries fill and stroke like any other shape; with neither, it draws nothing.
        if (!s.fill && !s.strokeColor) return '';
        return '<rect x="0" y="0" width="' + w + '" height="' + h + '" fill="' + fill + '"' + fillOpacity + stroke + '/>';
      default:
        return '';
    }
  }

  function connectorMarkup(s, ax, ay, bx, by) {
    var mid = 'm-' + s.id;
    // ONE marker, oriented auto-start-reverse, so it serves marker-start and marker-end both.
    // refX sits on the tip (viewBox x = 10), so the tip lands ON the endpoint -- mirroring
    // DrawingML's <a:headEnd> / <a:tailEnd>.
    var marker =
      '<defs><marker id="' + esc(mid) + '" viewBox="0 0 10 10" refX="10" refY="5" ' +
      'markerWidth="6" markerHeight="6" orient="auto-start-reverse" markerUnits="strokeWidth">' +
      '<path d="M 0 0 L 10 5 L 0 10 z" fill="' + esc(s.color) + '"/></marker></defs>';

    var mk = '';
    if (s.arrow === 'start' || s.arrow === 'both') mk += ' marker-start="url(#' + esc(mid) + ')"';
    if (s.arrow === 'end' || s.arrow === 'both') mk += ' marker-end="url(#' + esc(mid) + ')"';

    var attrs = 'fill="none" stroke="' + esc(s.color) + '" stroke-width="' + esc(s.width) + '"' + mk;

    if (s.kind === 'elbow') {
      // An APPROXIMATION. PowerPoint's MSO_CONNECTOR.ELBOW auto-routes; this polyline does not.
      // This is the one place the preview is knowingly inexact, and the UI says so.
      var mx = (ax + bx) / 2;
      return marker + '<polyline points="' + ax + ',' + ay + ' ' + mx + ',' + ay + ' ' +
             mx + ',' + by + ' ' + bx + ',' + by + '" ' + attrs + '/>';
    }
    return marker + '<line x1="' + ax + '" y1="' + ay + '" x2="' + bx + '" y2="' + by + '" ' + attrs + '/>';
  }

  function renderEl(el) {
    var s = read(el);
    var svg = el.querySelector('svg');

    if (s.type === 'connector') {
      // The wrapper spans the endpoints' AABB. max(..., 1) keeps a perfectly horizontal line's SVG
      // box non-degenerate and NEVER touches the IR, which stores the raw endpoints.
      var minX = Math.min(s.x1, s.x2), minY = Math.min(s.y1, s.y2);
      var w = Math.max(Math.abs(s.x2 - s.x1), 1);
      var h = Math.max(Math.abs(s.y2 - s.y1), 1);
      var ax = s.x1 - minX, ay = s.y1 - minY, bx = s.x2 - minX, by = s.y2 - minY;

      el.style.left = minX + 'px';
      el.style.top = minY + 'px';
      el.style.width = w + 'px';
      el.style.height = h + 'px';
      el.style.setProperty('--rot', '0deg');

      svg.setAttribute('width', w);
      svg.setAttribute('height', h);
      svg.setAttribute('viewBox', '0 0 ' + w + ' ' + h);
      svg.innerHTML = connectorMarkup(s, ax, ay, bx, by);

      var p1 = el.querySelector('.handle[data-h="p1"]');
      var p2 = el.querySelector('.handle[data-h="p2"]');
      if (p1) { p1.style.left = ax - 4 + 'px'; p1.style.top = ay - 4 + 'px'; }
      if (p2) { p2.style.left = bx - 4 + 'px'; p2.style.top = by - 4 + 'px'; }
      return;
    }
