      warn('The clipboard is not available',
        'This browser will not hand a script the clipboard. Use ?쏬oad JSON??to open a file instead.');
      return;
    }
    var text;
    try {
      text = await navigator.clipboard.readText();
    } catch (e) {
      warn('Could not read the clipboard',
        'The browser refused clipboard access ??grant this page clipboard permission, or use ' +
        '?쏬oad JSON??\n\n' + String(e));
      return;
    }
    if (!text || !text.trim()) {
      warn('The clipboard is empty', 'Copy the JSON from your chat first, then press Paste Gem JSON.');
      return;
    }
    var ir;
    try {
      ir = JSON.parse(stripFence(text));
    } catch (e) {
      warn('The clipboard is not JSON',
        'What was copied is not a canvas-ir/1 deck. Copy only the JSON block.\n\n' + String(e));
      return;
    }
    var before = snapshot();
    try {
      loadIR(ir);        // the SAME gate the file import and the live author go through
      pushUndo(before);  // it took: replacing the whole deck is ONE step, and it is reversible
      setStatus('Pasted ' + (ir.elements || []).length + ' element(s) from the clipboard.');
    } catch (e) {
      warn('That deck was refused ??the stage is unchanged', String(e.message || e));
      setStatus('Paste refused.');
    }
  }

  $('b-paste').addEventListener('click', pasteGemJSON);

  /* The mock/live badge. The MODE comes from /api/context, never inferred from behaviour. */
  function applyMode(mode, model) {
    var tag = $('ai-mode');
    var hint = $('ai-hint');
    if (mode === 'live') {
      if (tag) {
        tag.textContent = 'live';
        tag.classList.add('live');
        tag.title = model ? ('Model: ' + model) : 'A live model is called';
      }
      if (hint) {
        hint.innerHTML = 'Calls the configured model and <strong>replaces the stage</strong> with ' +
          'its draft (Ctrl+Z brings yours back). The draft is judged like any import.';
      }
    } else if (tag) {
      tag.textContent = 'mock';
      tag.classList.remove('live');
      tag.title = 'No model is called';
    }
  }

  /* Ctrl+Z inside the PROMPT is the browser's undo, not ours.
   *
   * The global handler runs undo/redo in FRONT of its editingText() guard on purpose ??so that undo
   * still works with the focus in the size box, which is exactly where the focus is right after the
   * edit you want to undo. This textarea is the one control where that is wrong: the user is typing
   * prose, they expect their keystrokes back, and what they would get instead is their whole deck
   * rolled back. Stop the event here rather than widen the global exception: the exception belongs
   * to this box, not to <input> at large. */
  $('p-prompt').addEventListener('keydown', function (ev) {
    var k = String(ev.key || '').toLowerCase();
    if ((ev.ctrlKey || ev.metaKey) && (k === 'z' || k === 'y')) ev.stopPropagation();
  });

  /* An IR element -> editor state.
   *
   * It NEVER throws and it NEVER repairs, and both halves of that are load-bearing.
   *
   * Not throwing is what lets the gate judge the WHOLE file and report every bad element at once,
   * instead of dying on the first one. `text`, `paragraphs`, `runs` and the first run are each
   * optional AND each hand-editable, so `text.paragraphs[0].runs[0]` -- which is what this used to
   * be -- is a TypeError in any file someone deleted a line from.
   *
   * Not repairing is what keeps the judge honest: a missing coordinate must arrive at
   * illegalReason() still missing, so that it is REFUSED, rather than quietly defaulted to 0 and
   * drawn somewhere the user never put it.
   */
  function stateFromIR(e) {
    if (!e || typeof e !== 'object') return { id: undefined, type: undefined };

    if (e.type === 'connector') {
      var cd = DEFAULTS.connector;
      return {
        id: e.id, type: 'connector',
        x1: e.x1, y1: e.y1, x2: e.x2, y2: e.y2,
        kind: e.kind || cd.kind,
        arrow: e.arrow || cd.arrow,
        color: e.color || cd.color,
        width: typeof e.width === 'number' ? e.width : cd.width,
        group: e.group !== undefined && e.group !== null ? e.group : '',
      };
    }

    // Media (Phase 6): geometry + src, nothing else -- no fill/stroke/text/rotation round-trips
    // for an image/video (see toIR()). Checked by literal string, not isMediaType(): this function
    // is lifted out of this file BY NAME and run in Node (tests/canvas/test_app.py), and a call out
    // to a helper the harness does not also lift is a ReferenceError in the gate, not in the code.
    if (e.type === 'image' || e.type === 'video') {
      return {
        id: e.id, type: e.type,
        x: e.x, y: e.y, w: e.w, h: e.h,
        rot: e.rotation === undefined || e.rotation === null ? 0 : e.rotation,
        src: typeof e.src === 'string' ? e.src : '',
        group: e.group !== undefined && e.group !== null ? e.group : '',
      };
    }

    var text = e.text && typeof e.text === 'object' ? e.text : {};
    var paras = Array.isArray(text.paragraphs) ? text.paragraphs : [];
    var runs0 = paras[0] && Array.isArray(paras[0].runs) ? paras[0].runs : [];
    var first = runs0[0] && typeof runs0[0] === 'object' ? runs0[0] : {};
    var def = e.type === 'textbox' ? DEFAULTS.textbox : DEFAULTS.shape;
    var stroke = e.stroke && typeof e.stroke === 'object' ? e.stroke : null;

    /* The element's bold/italic flags are a SUMMARY of the runs: on only when EVERY run is bold.
     *
     * Reading them off the FIRST run -- the way size and color are read -- would be wrong here, and
     * destructively so. The flag is OR-ed into every run on the way back out (normalizeText), so
     * "the first run is bold" would mean a deck with one bold word in a plain sentence reloads with
     * the flag on, and re-exports with the WHOLE sentence bold. A round trip must not change the
     * deck. Mixed runs therefore summarise to OFF, and their own <b>/<i> tags -- which
     * paragraphsToHTML() writes -- carry the per-run bold instead. Nothing is lost either way.
     *
     * Inlined rather than factored into a helper on purpose: this function is lifted out by name and
     * run in Node by tests/canvas/test_app.py, and a call to a helper that the harness does not also
     * lift is a ReferenceError in the gate rather than a failure in the code.
     */
    var nRuns = 0, nBold = 0, nItalic = 0;
    for (var pi = 0; pi < paras.length; pi++) {
      var prs = paras[pi] && Array.isArray(paras[pi].runs) ? paras[pi].runs : [];
      for (var ri = 0; ri < prs.length; ri++) {
        var r = prs[ri];
        if (!r || typeof r !== 'object') continue;
        nRuns += 1;
        if (r.bold) nBold += 1;
        if (r.italic) nItalic += 1;
      }
    }

    // Phase 6: split a #RRGGBBAA fill into its opaque colour and its alpha PERCENT (the inverse of
    // withAlpha() in toIR()) -- inlined rather than a shared helper for the same reason the
    // bold/italic summary above is: stateFromIR() is lifted by name into a harness that does not
    // also lift withAlpha()/hex2(), and a call out to either would be a ReferenceError there, not a
    // failure in the code. A plain #RRGGBB (or no fill at all) is unaffected: fillAlpha is 100.
    var fillRaw = typeof e.fill === 'string' ? e.fill : '';
    var fillColor = fillRaw;
    var fillAlpha = 100;
    if (/^#[0-9A-Fa-f]{8}$/.test(fillRaw)) {
      fillColor = fillRaw.slice(0, 7);
      fillAlpha = Math.round((parseInt(fillRaw.slice(7, 9), 16) / 255) * 100);
    }

    return {
      id: e.id, type: e.type,
      x: e.x, y: e.y, w: e.w, h: e.h,
      // Absent or null rotation is 0. A rotation of "45" is NOT 45: it stays a string, and the judge
      // refuses it -- exactly as validate.py::_has_readable_geometry does on the other side.
      rot: e.rotation === undefined || e.rotation === null ? 0 : e.rotation,
      fill: fillColor,
      fillAlpha: fillAlpha,
      strokeColor: (stroke && stroke.color) || '',
      strokeWidth: stroke && typeof stroke.width === 'number' ? stroke.width : 1,
      // A textless element carries no run to learn from, so fall back to the DEFAULT FOR ITS
      // TYPE -- black for a textbox, white for a shape. Defaulting both to white would hand a
      // reloaded textbox white-on-nothing text.
      size: first.size || def.size,
      color: first.color || def.color,
      align: text.align || def.align,
      valign: text.valign || def.valign,
      bold: nRuns > 0 && nBold === nRuns,
      italic: nRuns > 0 && nItalic === nRuns,
      group: e.group !== undefined && e.group !== null ? e.group : '',
    };
  }

  /* THE SECOND CALLER OF THE JUDGE, and the reason the judge exists as its own function.
   *
   * ALL OR NOTHING, and in this order: judge every element FIRST, touch the stage SECOND.
   *
   * A file is not a trusted input just because this editor may have written it -- it is a
   * hand-editable text file, and the moment one of its elements could come to rest on the logo,
   * "the reserved regions are strictly enforced" stopped being a true sentence. They were enforced
   * against the mouse and not against a file.
   *
   * The ORDER is the other half of the fix, and it is not cosmetic. This function used to clear the
   * stage and overwrite the title before it had looked at a single element, so a per-element check
   * bolted into the loop -- however correct that check was -- could only ever leave a half-imported
   * stage behind and the user's own deck already gone. "Reject the entire import" is not reachable
   * from inside that loop. It is reachable from here.
   */
  function loadIR(ir) {
    if (!ir || typeof ir !== 'object') throw new Error('that file does not contain a deck');
    if (ir.schema !== SCHEMA) throw new Error('schema is ' + ir.schema + ', expected ' + SCHEMA);
    if (!ir.canvas || ir.canvas.w !== CANVAS.w || ir.canvas.h !== CANVAS.h) {
      throw new Error('that deck was authored in a different model space; it is never rescaled');
    }
    var raw = ir.elements === undefined || ir.elements === null ? [] : ir.elements;
    if (!Array.isArray(raw)) throw new Error('elements is not a list');

    /* PASS 1 -- JUDGE. Nothing below this line has touched the stage, and nothing above it may. */
    var states = [];
    var problems = [];
    var seen = Object.create(null); // no prototype, so an id of "__proto__" is just a key
    for (var i = 0; i < raw.length; i++) {
      var s = stateFromIR(raw[i]);
      states.push(s);

      // The same label validate.py prints, so the editor's refusal and the backend's 422 read as
      // one voice rather than as two tools that happen to disagree with your file.
      var at = 'elements[' + i + '] (id "' + (typeof s.id === 'string' ? s.id : '?') +
               '", type "' + (typeof s.type === 'string' ? s.type : '?') + '")';

      var why = illegalReason(s);
      if (why) problems.push(at + ': ' + why);
      if (typeof s.group !== 'string') {
        problems.push(at + ': group must be a string');
      }

      // Ids become shape NAMES in the .pptx and the round-trip suite finds shapes by name, so two
      // elements sharing one makes one of them unfindable. validate.py rejects it; so do we.
      if (typeof s.id !== 'string' || !s.id) {
        problems.push(at + ': has no id -- an id becomes the shape name in the .pptx');
      } else if (s.id in seen) {
        problems.push(at + ': duplicate id -- already used by elements[' + seen[s.id] + ']');
      } else {
        seen[s.id] = i;
      }
    }

    if (problems.length) {
      throw new Error(
        problems.length + ' element(s) in that file may not be placed on this stage, so NOTHING ' +
        'was loaded and the deck you had is untouched:\n' +
        problems.map(function (p, n) { return '  ' + (n + 1) + '. ' + p; }).join('\n')
      );
    }

    /* PASS 2 -- COMMIT. Every state is legal, so this cannot fail half-way through. */
    [].slice.call(stage.querySelectorAll(':scope > .el')).forEach(function (n) { n.remove(); });
    select(null);
    titleField.textContent = ir.title || '';

    for (var j = 0; j < states.length; j++) {
      var el = makeEl(states[j]);
      stage.appendChild(el); // array order IS z-order -- preserve the file's order exactly
      var body = el.querySelector('.txt .body');
      var txt = raw[j] && typeof raw[j].text === 'object' ? raw[j].text : null;
      if (body && txt) body.innerHTML = paragraphsToHTML(txt.paragraphs, states[j].color);
    }
    setStatus('Loaded ' + states.length + ' element(s).');
  }

  /* Runs are hand-editable too. A paragraph with no `runs`, or a run with no `t`, is a file someone
   * edited -- not a file we wrote -- and it must not be a TypeError. It used to be one, and it was
   * thrown AFTER the stage had already been cleared.
   */
  function paragraphsToHTML(paragraphs, defColor) {
    var list = Array.isArray(paragraphs) ? paragraphs : [];
    return list.map(function (p) {
      var runs = p && Array.isArray(p.runs) ? p.runs : [];
      var inner = runs.map(function (r) {
        if (!r || typeof r !== 'object') return '';
        var raw = r.t === undefined || r.t === null ? '' : r.t;
        var t = esc(raw).replace(/\n/g, '<br>');
        if (r.bold) t = '<b>' + t + '</b>';
        if (r.italic) t = '<i>' + t + '</i>';
        // A run with no colour inherits the element's, and it does so EXPLICITLY: normalizeText()
        // reads the colour back off this span on the way out, so leaving it empty would round-trip
        // the colour as the string "undefined" and lose it.
        return '<span style="color: ' + esc(r.color || defColor) + '">' + t + '</span>';
      }).join('');
      return '<div>' + inner + '</div>';
    }).join('');
  }

  /* [REDACTED] start-up */

  function disableExports(why) {
    $('b-export').disabled = true;
    $('b-json').disabled = true;
    $('b-view-json').disabled = true; // same guard as #b-json: serialize() needs CONTEXT
    // The draft comes from the same server. Leaving it live would offer a button whose only possible
    // outcome is the banner the user is already reading.
    $('b-generate').disabled = true;
    setStatus(why);
  }

  async function loadContext() {
    try {
      var res = await fetch('/api/context');
      if (!res.ok) throw new Error('HTTP ' + res.status);
      CONTEXT = await res.json();
      applyMode(CONTEXT.mode, CONTEXT.model);
    } catch (e) {
      CONTEXT = null;
      disableExports('Offline ??export disabled.');
      warn(
        'Cannot reach the server ??Export and Download JSON are disabled',
        'The template SHA comes from /api/context and is NEVER hard-coded here: a payload carrying ' +
        'a fabricated SHA is worse than no payload (the renderer would reject it with a 409, or ' +
        'worse, accept it against a template it was not authored for). Start the server with ' +
        '`python -m aippt.canvas.app` and reload.\n\n' + String(e)
      );
      return;
    }

    // The server is the authority on the model space too. If it disagrees with us, we are the
    // stale one, and we must not author a payload it will only reject.
    var c = CONTEXT.canvas || {};
    if (c.w !== CANVAS.w || c.h !== CANVAS.h || c.unit !== CANVAS.unit) {
      CONTEXT = null;
      disableExports('Model space mismatch ??export disabled.');
      warn(
        'This editor and the server disagree about the model space',
        'The editor speaks ' + JSON.stringify(CANVAS) + ' and the server speaks ' +
        JSON.stringify(c) + '. A payload is NEVER rescaled. Hard-refresh the page; if it persists, ' +
        'the editor build is stale.'
      );
    }
  }

  async function checkFont() {
    await document.fonts.ready;
    if (!document.fonts.check('14pt "Century Gothic"')) {
      warn(
        'Century Gothic is not installed. This preview does NOT match what PowerPoint will render.',
        'Text metrics, wrapping and overflow on this stage are being measured in a substitute ' +
        'typeface. The exported deck will still ask for Century Gothic (latin) and 留묒? 怨좊뵓 (EA), ' +
        'so what you see here is not what your audience will see. A preview that lies silently is ' +
        'worse than no preview.'
      );
    }
  }

  function fileToBase64(file) {
    return new Promise(function (resolve, reject) {
      var reader = new FileReader();
      reader.onload = function () {
        var res = reader.result;
        resolve(res.substring(res.indexOf(',') + 1));
      };
      reader.onerror = reject;
      reader.readAsDataURL(file);
    });
  }

  async function init() {
    // Emit <b>/<i> rather than styled spans. We handle both anyway, but tags are what the run model
    // actually wants.
    try { document.execCommand('styleWithCSS', false, false); } catch (e) { /* not fatal */ }

    setTool('select');
    setZoom(100);
    syncProps();    // nothing is selected, so this HIDES the panel rather than filling it
    syncHistory();  // ...and an empty stack disables Undo and Redo
    await loadContext();
    await checkFont();
  }

  init();
})();

===== tasks\AI_Powered_PPT\src\aippt\canvas\static\interactions.js (5982 bytes) =====
/* interactions.js ??drag / resize / free-drag on pointer capture.
 *
 * THIS IS THE SANCTIONED FALLBACK, not a vendored interact.js. Plan Sec 5.1 offers exactly two
 * options -- "interact.js (~10 KB) ... or ~150 lines of pointerdown/pointermove/pointerup with
 * pointer capture" -- and this is the second. The reason is recorded in phase1b_change_detail.md;
 * the short version is that a CDN is not reachable at runtime on this network, an npm build step is
 * out of scope, and vendoring a minified blob we cannot fetch is not an option.
 *
 * IT IS DELIBERATELY interact.js-SHAPED. Call sites use `Interactions.draggable(sel, {listeners})`
 * and `.resizable(...)`, and the move event carries `dx`/`dy` and `deltaRect` with interact.js's
 * meanings and interact.js's units. Dropping the real library in later is therefore a rewrite of
 * this one file and nothing else.
 *
 * WHAT IT DOES NOT DO, ON PURPOSE:
 *
 *   - It never hands the caller a MEASURED rectangle. interact.js exposes one on its move event --
 *     a device-pixel box taken straight from the DOM -- and consuming it silently reintroduces
 *     every bug the Prime Directive exists to delete: it breaks the moment zoom != 1. Not offering
 *     it at all is strictly better than offering it and asking people not to touch it. This file
 *     measures nothing; it only ever DIFFERENCES two pointer positions.
 *   - It has no `snap` and no `restrict` modifiers. interact.js's operate in page/device pixels, so
 *     at zoom = 0.5 a 4-unit grid quietly becomes a 2-unit grid and a reserved-region restriction
 *     lands in the wrong place. ALL snapping and ALL constraint enforcement happen in model space,
 *     in editor.js, after the / zoom conversion.
 *
 * Units: dx, dy and every deltaRect field are DEVICE PIXELS -- exactly as interact.js reports them.
 * The caller divides by `zoom`. That division is the caller's job and it is grep-enforced (Gate 1).
 */

(function (global) {
  'use strict';

  // data-h -> which edges of the box this handle moves. Mirrors interact.js's `edges` option.
  var EDGES = {
    nw: { left: true, top: true },
    n: { top: true },
    ne: { right: true, top: true },
    e: { right: true },
    se: { right: true, bottom: true },
    s: { bottom: true },
    sw: { left: true, bottom: true },
    w: { left: true },
  };

  /* The one primitive. Everything below is a thin wrapper on it.
   *
   * Pointer capture is what makes this robust: once the pointer is captured, the moves keep
   * arriving at the captured node even when the cursor outruns the element, leaves the stage, or
   * crosses an iframe. That is the entire reason the plan names pointer capture specifically
   * rather than "mousemove on document".
   */
  function pointerDrag(node, opts) {
    node.addEventListener('pointerdown', function (ev) {
      if (ev.button !== 0) return; // primary button only
      if (opts.filter && !opts.filter(ev)) return;

      ev.preventDefault();
      ev.stopPropagation();

      var last = { x: ev.clientX, y: ev.clientY };
      var ctx = { target: node, pointer: ev, dx: 0, dy: 0 };

      // Capture on the node that received the event, so a re-render of a SIBLING (the <svg>, which
      // editor.js does replace every frame) cannot pull the captured node out from under us.
      node.setPointerCapture(ev.pointerId);

      if (opts.start) opts.start(ctx);

      function onMove(mv) {
        if (mv.pointerId !== ev.pointerId) return;
        ctx.pointer = mv;
        ctx.dx = mv.clientX - last.x; // device px. The caller divides by zoom.
        ctx.dy = mv.clientY - last.y;
        last.x = mv.clientX;
        last.y = mv.clientY;
        if (opts.move) opts.move(ctx);
      }

      function onUp(up) {
        if (up.pointerId !== ev.pointerId) return;
        node.removeEventListener('pointermove', onMove);
        node.removeEventListener('pointerup', onUp);
        node.removeEventListener('pointercancel', onUp);
        if (node.hasPointerCapture(up.pointerId)) node.releasePointerCapture(up.pointerId);
        ctx.pointer = up;
        if (opts.end) opts.end(ctx);
      }

      node.addEventListener('pointermove', onMove);
      node.addEventListener('pointerup', onUp);
      node.addEventListener('pointercancel', onUp);
    });
  }

  var Interactions = {
    pointerDrag: pointerDrag,

    /* Drag the whole element. `ignoreFrom` is a selector: a pointerdown starting inside it is not a
     * drag (the text box, so a click lands the caret; the handles, so they resize instead).
     * `enabled` is handed the triggering pointerdown event (Phase 6: editor.js reads
     * ev.shiftKey off it to let a Shift+Click toggle multi-selection instead of starting a
     * drag) -- existing callers that declared `enabled: function () {...}` and ignore the
     * argument are unaffected. */
    draggable: function (el, opts) {
      pointerDrag(el, {
        filter: function (ev) {
          if (opts.ignoreFrom && ev.target.closest(opts.ignoreFrom)) return false;
          return !opts.enabled || opts.enabled(ev);
        },
        start: opts.start,
        move: opts.move,
        end: opts.end,
      });
    },

    /* Resize from a handle. The move event carries `deltaRect`, in device px, with interact.js's
     * exact semantics: how the box's left/top/width/height changed THIS FRAME. */
    resizable: function (handle, edgeKey, opts) {
      var edges = EDGES[edgeKey] || {};
      pointerDrag(handle, {
        start: opts.start,
        move: function (ctx) {
          ctx.edges = edges;
          ctx.deltaRect = {
            left: edges.left ? ctx.dx : 0,
            top: edges.top ? ctx.dy : 0,
            width: (edges.right ? ctx.dx : 0) - (edges.left ? ctx.dx : 0),
            height: (edges.bottom ? ctx.dy : 0) - (edges.top ? ctx.dy : 0),
          };
          opts.move(ctx);
        },
        end: opts.end,
      });
    },
  };

  global.Interactions = Interactions;
})(window);

  352: async def api_generate(request: Request) -> Response:
  353:     """{"prompt", "model"?, "images"?} in, canvas-ir/1 out. MOCK without AIPPT_LIVE_MODE=1, LIVE with it.
  354: 
  355:     Either way the editor hands what comes back straight to loadIR(), which judges every element
  356:     before it touches the stage -- so a model draft is no more trusted than a hand-edited file. The
  357:     request shape, the loading state and the hand-off are identical in both modes; only the author
  358:     behind the seam changes.
  359: 
  360:     `prompt` IS STILL THE ONLY MANDATORY FIELD (Phase 1). `{"prompt": "..."}` on its own means what
  361:     it has always meant: the default profile, no attachments. The two new fields are additive.
  362: 
  363:     EVERY CHECK RUNS BEFORE THE MODE BRANCH. A body naming a profile that does not exist, or one
  364:     carrying an image that is not one, is refused identically on a laptop with AIPPT_LIVE_MODE unset
  365:     and on one with it set -- so a client bug cannot hide behind mock mode until it reaches
  366:     production. It also means those refusals cost neither the mock's 1.5 s nor a CLI round trip.
  367:     """
  368:     body, err = await _read_json(request)
  369:     if err is not None:
  370:         return err
  371: 
  372:     prompt = body.get("prompt") if isinstance(body, dict) else None
  373:     if not isinstance(prompt, str) or not prompt.strip():
  374:         # Refused BEFORE any model call or sleep. No author would have been invoked for an empty body.
  375:         return JSONResponse(
  376:             {"error": "validation", "detail": 'the body must be {"prompt": "<non-empty text>"}'},
  377:             status_code=422,
  378:         )
  379: 
  380:     profile, err = _resolve_profile(body.get("model"))
  381:     if err is not None:
  382:         return err
  383: 
  384:     images, err = _decode_images(body.get("images"))
  385:     if err is not None:
  386:         return err
  387: 
  388:     pin = request.app.state.pin
  389: 
  390:     # --- MOCK: AIPPT_LIVE_MODE is not set. The prompt is NOT read; a fixed deck comes back 1.5 s late.
  391:     if not live_mode_enabled():
  392:         # Logged BY LENGTH, never by content: this is an unauthenticated local endpoint and the deck
  393:         # text is the user's, not ours.
  394:         #
  395:         # THE PROFILE AND THE IMAGES WERE VALIDATED AND ARE NOW DELIBERATELY DROPPED. A mock that
  396:         # varied with either would stop being a mock: [REDACTED]
  397:         # requires two different requests to produce byte-identical decks, and that property is the
  398:         # only reason the mock is worth having.
  399:         log.info(
  400:             "mock generate: %d-char prompt, %d image(s), profile %s (none of it is read -- "
  401:             "no model is called)",
  402:             len(prompt), len(images), profile,
  403:         )
  404:         ir = mock_ir(pin)
  405: 
  406:         # THE MOCK IS VALIDATED BEFORE IT IS SERVED, by the same validator the export path runs. It
  407:         # cannot fail because of anything the caller sent -- so if it fails, it is OUR bug: a 500.
  408:         try:
  409:             validate(ir)
  410:         except CanvasValidationError as exc:
  411:             log.exception("the MOCK payload does not validate -- a server bug, not a user error")
  412:             return JSONResponse({"error": "mock", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=500)
  413: 
  414:         await asyncio.sleep(MOCK_LATENCY_S)
  415:         return JSONResponse(ir)
  416: 
  417:     # --- LIVE: a real CLI-worker call. The prompt IS read now. [REDACTED]
  418:     cfg = MODEL_PROFILES[profile]
  419:     log.info(
  420:         "live generate: %d-char prompt, %d image(s) (validated, not forwarded -- see "
  421:         "generate_live()), profile %s (%s thinking) -> backend=%s model=%s",
  422:         len(prompt), len(images), profile, cfg["thinking"], cfg["backend"], cfg["model"],
  423:     )
  424:     try:
  425:         ir = await generate_live(prompt.strip(), pin, profile=profile, images=images)
  426:     except LiveGenerateError as exc:
  427:         log.warning("live generate failed: %s", exc)
  428:         return JSONResponse({"error": "generate", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=502)
  429: 
  430:     # The assembled deck goes through the SAME validator the export path runs. A model can put a box
  431:     # on the logo as easily as a human can; refusing it here means the editor is never handed a draft
  432:     # it could only bounce, and the detail is the same numbered list validate() gives everywhere.
  433:     try:
  434:         validate(ir)
  435:     except CanvasValidationError as exc:
  436:         return JSONResponse({"error": "model", "detail": str(exc)}, status_code=502)
  437: 
  438:     return JSONResponse(ir)
  439: 
  440: 
  441: @app.get("/api/designs")
  442: async def api_list_designs() -> dict[str, Any]:
  443:     """Every design saved so far, by name. AI_PPT_v2 Phase 4.
  444: 
  445:     Sorted so the editor's dropdown does not reorder itself between one load and the next just
  446:     because the filesystem happened to iterate `saved_decks/` in a different order this time.
  447:     """
  448:     names = sorted(p.stem for p in SAVED_DECKS_DIR.glob("*.json") if p.is_file())
  449:     return {"designs": names}
  450: 
  451: 
  452: @app.post("/api/designs")
  453: async def api_save_design(request: Request) -> Response:
  454:     """{"name", "ir"} in, the design written to disk. AI_PPT_v2 Phase 4.
  455: 
  456:     THE ONE ROUTE THAT WRITES A FILE, and it is deliberately narrow: `ir` goes through the SAME
  457:     validate() /api/export runs, so a saved design can never be a deck this server would later
  458:     refuse to load back -- and `name` goes through _validate_design_name()/_design_path(), so the
  459:     write can only ever land inside SAVED_DECKS_DIR under a name matching the allowlist.
  460:     """
  461:     body, err = await _read_json(request)
  462:     if err is not None:
  463:         return err
  464:     if not isinstance(body, dict):
  465:         return _rejected(
  466:             "body_shape", 'the body must be {"name": "<string>", "ir": <canvas-ir/1 object>}'
  467:         )
  468: 
  469:     name, err = _validate_design_name(body.get("name"))
  470:     if err is not None:
  746: # --- the live author (Phase 4; repointed at CliWorkerClient by the headless-worker migration) ----
  747: #
  748: # AIPPT_LIVE_MODE picks the mode. Everything here is ordinary, testable code EXCEPT
  749: # CliWorkerClient.complete_json() itself, the one seam that shells out to a claude/codex CLI (see
  750: # aippt.adapters.ai_adapter) -- the mocking test monkeypatches exactly that method and exercises all
  751: # the assembly (schema relaxation, prompt injection, pin injection, validation) around it for real.
  752: 
  753: 
  754: def live_mode_enabled() -> bool:
  755:     """True only for the literal value "1" -- not merely truthy. Unset, empty, "0" and "false" all
  756:     mean mock; there is no ambiguous middle value a shell profile could leave behind by accident."""
  757:     return os.environ.get("AIPPT_LIVE_MODE", "").strip() == "1"
  758: 
  759: 
  760: def server_mode() -> str:
  761:     """'live' when AIPPT_LIVE_MODE=1, else 'mock'. The editor reads this from /api/context and
  762:     flips its badge; it never infers the mode from behaviour."""
  763:     return "live" if live_mode_enabled() else "mock"
  764: 
  765: 
  766: def system_instruction() -> str:
  767:     try:
  768:         return PROMPT_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
  769:     except OSError as exc:
  770:         raise LiveGenerateError(
  771:             f"the system prompt could not be read at {PROMPT_PATH}: {exc}"
  772:         ) from exc
  773: 
  774: 
  775: @lru_cache(maxsize=1)
  776: def _relaxed_live_schema() -> dict[str, Any]:
  777:     """canvas/schema.json (the SAME file validate.py enforces at export time) with `target` dropped
  778:     from the top-level `required` list -- everything else, including `target`'s own inner schema for
  779:     when a model DOES include it, stays exactly as strict.
  780: 
  781:     The model cannot know reference/template_pin.json's current SHA or the pinned layout index --
  782:     both are injected from the LIVE pin by _finalize_live_ir() after the call returns, never
  783:     authored by the model. Relaxing anything beyond this one field would let a model skip work
  784:     _finalize_live_ir() does not do on its behalf.
  785:     """
  786:     schema = json.loads(SCHEMA_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
  787:     schema["required"] = [name for name in schema["required"] if name != "target"]
  788:     return schema
  789: 
  790: 
  791: def _finalize_live_ir(data: Any, pin: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
  792:     """Overwrite schema, canvas and target from the LIVE PIN -- the model is never trusted with them.
  793: 
  794:     The system prompt tells the model to omit target entirely (it has no business choosing our
  795:     template SHA) and to fix schema and canvas to constants. We ENFORCE all three here rather than
  796:     hope: a model that ignores the instruction does not get to author the pin. Everything else --
  797:     title and elements -- is the model's, and validate() judges it next.
  798:     """
  799:     if not isinstance(data, dict):
  800:         raise LiveGenerateError(f"the model returned a {type(data).__name__}, not a JSON object")
  801:     ir = dict(data)
  802:     ir["schema"] = "canvas-ir/1"
  803:     ir["canvas"] = dict(CANVAS)
  804:     ir["target"] = {
  805:         "template": pin["renderer_template"]["filename"],
  806:         "template_sha256": pin["renderer_template"]["sha256"],
  807:         "layout_index": pin["layout_signature"]["layout_index"],
  808:     }
  809:     ir.setdefault("title", "")
  810:     if not isinstance(ir.get("elements"), list):
  811:         ir["elements"] = []
  812: 
  813:     # Robustness fallback: Models often omit `color` on runs that are just whitespace (" ").
  814:     # The schema requires `color` on EVERY run. We inject a default to prevent failing the whole deck.
  815:     for el in ir["elements"]:
  816:         if isinstance(el, dict):
  817:             # Connector arrow fallback: The schema requires `arrow` on connectors.
  818:             # Models sometimes omit it for simple lines. Default to "none".
  819:             if el.get("type") == "connector" and "arrow" not in el:
  820:                 el["arrow"] = "none"
  821: 
  822:             if isinstance(el.get("text"), dict):
  823:                 for p in el["text"].get("paragraphs", []):
  824:                     if isinstance(p, dict) and isinstance(p.get("runs"), list):
  825:                         for run in p["runs"]:
  826:                             if isinstance(run, dict) and "color" not in run:
  827:                                 run["color"] = "#1A1A1A"
  828: 
  829:     return ir
  830: 
  831: 
  832: async def generate_live(
  833:     prompt: str,
  834:     pin: dict[str, Any],
  835:     profile: str = DEFAULT_PROFILE,
  836:     images: list[dict[str, Any]] | None = None,
  837: ) -> dict[str, Any]:
  838:     """prompt (+ profile) -> canvas-ir/1 for the live author. LiveGenerateError on any failure.
  839: 
  840:     IMAGES ARE VALIDATED BUT NEVER FORWARDED. CliWorkerClient.complete_json() (aippt.adapters.
  841:     ai_adapter) is a text-only seam -- system + user strings in, a schema-checked dict out -- with no
  842:     attachment parameter at all. `images` stays in this function's signature so the request contract
  843:     with the editor and with api_generate()'s _decode_images() limits does not change, but nothing in
  844:     it reaches the model. A caller who needs vision input is not servable by this seam yet; that is a
  845:     capability gap, not a silently dropped validation -- oversized or malformed attachments are still
  846:     refused, before this function is ever called.
  847: 
  848:     CliWorkerClient.complete_json() IS SYNCHRONOUS -- it shells out to a claude/codex CLI via
  849:     subprocess.run() and can block for up to its timeout_s (300 s by default). Calling it directly on
  850:     this coroutine would stall the entire single-threaded event loop for as long as the model takes
  851:     to answer, freezing every other request in flight (export included). asyncio.to_thread() runs it
  852:     on a worker thread instead so the loop stays free.
  853: 
  854:     JSON EXTRACTION AND SCHEMA VALIDATION ALREADY HAPPENED by the time this call returns --
  855:     complete_json() only ever answers with a dict that already matches the schema it was given, or
  856:     raises. There is no code-fence-stripping or json.loads() step here any more; that responsibility
  857:     moved into ai_adapter.py's _extract_json_object()/_validate_against_schema(), which is what the
  858:     CliWorkerClient.complete_json contract exists to guarantee.
  859:     """
  860:     del images  # accepted for signature/contract compatibility only -- see docstring above
  861: 
  862:     cfg = MODEL_PROFILES[profile]
  863:     client = CliWorkerClient(track="b", backend=cfg["backend"], model=cfg["model"], timeout_s=900)
  864: 
  865:     try:
  866:         obj = await asyncio.to_thread(
  867:             client.complete_json,
  868:             system=system_instruction(),
  869:             user=prompt,
  870:             schema=_relaxed_live_schema(),
  871:         )
  872:     except CliWorkerError as exc:
  873:         raise LiveGenerateError(f"the model call failed: {exc}") from exc
  874: 
  875:     return _finalize_live_ir(obj, pin)
  876: 
  877: 
  878: def content_disposition(title: str) -> str:
  879:     """RFC 5987, because the title is Korean often enough that ASCII-only is simply wrong.
  880: 
  881:     Two filenames go out: a sanitized ASCII `filename=` that ancient clients can read, and a
  882:     percent-encoded UTF-8 `filename*=` that every current browser prefers. A Korean title collapses
  883:     to nothing under ASCII folding, so the fallback degrades to "deck.pptx" rather than to an empty
  884:     or a mangled name.
  885:     """
  886:     stem = _UNSAFE_STEM.sub(" ", title).strip()
  887:     stem = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", stem)[:80].strip() or DEFAULT_STEM
  888: 
  889:     # NFKD then drop the non-ASCII: "Caf챕" -> "Cafe", "?뚯쓽 ?먮즺" -> "" -> DEFAULT_STEM.
  890:     folded = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", stem).encode("ascii", "ignore").decode("ascii").strip()
  891:     ascii_stem = folded or DEFAULT_STEM
  892: 
  893:     encoded = urllib.parse.quote(f"{stem}.pptx", safe="")
  894:     return f"attachment; filename=\"{ascii_stem}.pptx\"; filename*=UTF-8''{encoded}"
  895: 
  896: 
  897: # --- the mock author (Phase 3) [REDACTED]
  898: #
  899: # Three cards and two arrows, laid out inside BODY_SAFE (45.6, 92 -> 914.4, 500). The numbers are
  900: # stated once, here, so that "why is that card at 656?" has an answer you can read instead of
  901: # arithmetic you have to redo.
  902: 
  903: _CARD_Y = 232.0
  904: _CARD_W = 244.0
  905: _CARD_H = 116.0
  906: _CARD_X = (60.0, 358.0, 656.0)     # 656 + 244 == 900, clear of BODY_SAFE's right edge (914.4)
  907: _MID_Y = _CARD_Y + _CARD_H / 2.0   # the arrows leave and arrive at the cards' vertical centre
  908: 
  909: _PAPER = "#FFFFFF"
  910: _INK = "#1A1A1A"
  911: _MUTED = "#6B727C"
  912: 
  913: _CARDS = (
  914:     ("card_awareness", _CARD_X[0], "Awareness", "?좉퇋 梨꾨꼸 3怨?쨌 釉뚮옖???몄???+18%"),
  915:     ("card_consider", _CARD_X[1], "Consideration", "?곕え ?붿껌 1.4k嫄?쨌 肄섑뀗痢?泥대쪟 2.3諛?),
  509: # Neither call track below ever puts free-form prompt text (topic, sources, the full
  510: # layout catalog) on the command line -- it goes into a temp file, and the CLI is told
  511: # only "read this file". That sidesteps three problems at once: OS argv length limits,
  512: # `claude`/`codex` on Windows commonly resolving to a `.cmd` shim (which cmd.exe
  513: # re-parses for shell metacharacters when invoked), and needing to escape arbitrary
  514: # user content for a shell we would otherwise rather not invoke at all.
  515: 
  516: 
  517: class CliWorkerError(RuntimeError):
  518:     """A headless CLI worker call failed, timed out, or returned unusable output."""
  519: 
  520: 
  521: class CliWorkerSchemaError(CliWorkerError):
  522:     """The CLI returned parseable JSON that does not conform to the requested schema.
  523: 
  524:     Kept distinct from ``CliWorkerError`` so a caller's repair loop (``generate_deck``)
  525:     can tell "the model answered but got the shape wrong -- repairable by re-prompting"
  526:     apart from "the CLI call itself failed -- not repairable that way".
  527:     """
  528: 
  529: 
  530: class CliWorkerClientError(CliWorkerError):
  531:     """``CliWorkerClient`` refused to build a request because a value it was about
  532:     to embed unvalidated (e.g. into generated YAML front matter) was unsafe.
  533:     """
  534: 
  535: 
  536: def _find_dotenv(filename: str = ".env") -> Path | None:
  537:     for base in (Path(__file__).resolve().parent, Path.cwd()):
  538:         for parent in (base, *base.parents):
  539:             candidate = parent / filename
  540:             if candidate.is_file():
  541:                 return candidate
  542:     return None
  543: 
  544: 
  545: _dotenv_loaded = False
  546: 
  547: 
  548: def _load_dotenv_once() -> None:
  549:     """Populate ``os.environ`` from ``AI_Powered_PPT/.env``, without clobbering
  550:     variables the caller already set explicitly. Prefers ``python-dotenv`` if it
  551:     happens to be installed; otherwise falls back to a small parser so this module
  552:     carries no hard dependency on it.
  553:     """
  554:     global _dotenv_loaded
  555:     if _dotenv_loaded:
  556:         return
  557:     _dotenv_loaded = True
  558:     dotenv_path = _find_dotenv()
  559:     if dotenv_path is None:
  560:         return
  561:     try:
  562:         from dotenv import load_dotenv  # type: ignore
  563: 
  564:         load_dotenv(dotenv_path, override=False)
  565:         return
  566:     except ImportError:
  567:         pass
  568:     for raw_line in dotenv_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
  569:         line = raw_line.strip()
  570:         if not line or line.startswith("#") or "=" not in line:
  571:             continue
  572:         key, _, value = line.partition("=")
  573:         key = key.strip()
  574:         value = value.strip()
  575:         if len(value) >= 2 and value[0] == value[-1] and value[0] in "\"'":
  576:             value = value[1:-1]
  577:         os.environ.setdefault(key, value)
  578: 
  579: 
  580: def _multiagent_root() -> str | None:
  581:     """``MULTIAGENT_ROOT``, from the environment or ``AI_Powered_PPT/.env`` -- never
  582:     hardcoded, since it names a machine-local MultiAgent staging path (Track A's
  583:     dispatcher lives under it; Track B uses it only as a working directory, and
  584:     works without it).
  585:     """
  586:     _load_dotenv_once()
  587:     return os.environ.get("MULTIAGENT_ROOT") or None
  588: 
  589: 
  590: def _require_multiagent_root() -> str:
  591:     root = _multiagent_root()
  592:     if not root:
  593:         raise CliWorkerError(
  594:             "MULTIAGENT_ROOT is not set. Add it to tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/.env, e.g.\n"
  595:             '    MULTIAGENT_ROOT="/c/Users/<you>/.ma-stg/stg.<id>"\n'
  596:             "or export it before constructing CliWorkerClient(track='a')."
  597:         )
  598:     return root
  599: 
  600: 
  601: def _resolve_executable(name: str) -> str:
  602:     resolved = shutil.which(name)
  603:     if resolved is None:
  604:         raise CliWorkerError(
  605:             f"{name!r} not found on PATH -- install/log in to the CLI first; "
  606:             "headless calls cannot complete an interactive auth flow."
  607:         )
  608:     return resolved
  609: 
  610: 
  611: def _run_cli(cmd: list[str], *, cwd: str | None, timeout_s: int, input_str: str | None = None) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]:
  612:     """``subprocess.run`` with controlled stdin/stdout and, on Windows
  613:     only, ``shell=True`` so a ``.cmd``/``.bat`` shim (common for npm-installed CLIs)
  614:     actually launches -- ``CreateProcess`` cannot exec a batch file directly, only
  615:     ``cmd.exe`` can. Safe to do unconditionally here specifically because every
  616:     caller keeps free-form content out of argv (see module note above); nothing
  617:     reaches the shell that this module didn't generate itself.
  618:     """
  619:     env = {**os.environ, "CI": "1"}
  620:     use_shell = os.name == "nt" and cmd[0].lower().endswith((".cmd", ".bat"))
  621:     run_kwargs = {}
  622:     if input_str is not None:
  623:         run_kwargs["input"] = input_str
  624:     else:
  625:         run_kwargs["stdin"] = subprocess.DEVNULL
  626: 
  627:     return subprocess.run(
  628:         cmd,
  629:         cwd=cwd,
  630:         env=env,
  631:         capture_output=True,
  632:         text=True,
  633:         encoding="utf-8",
  634:         errors="replace",
  635:         timeout=timeout_s,
  636:         shell=use_shell,
  637:         **run_kwargs,
  638:     )
  639: 
  640: 
  641: def _write_prompt_file(prompt: str) -> Path:
  642:     fd, name = tempfile.mkstemp(prefix="cliworker_brief_", suffix=".md")
  643:     path = Path(name)
  644:     try:
  645:         with os.fdopen(fd, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
  646:             f.write(prompt)
  647:     except Exception:
  648:         path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
  649:         raise
  650:     return path
  651: 
  652: 
  653: def _file_prompt_wrapper(brief_path: Path) -> str:
  654:     return (
  655:         "Read the worker brief file at this exact path and follow it exactly:\n"
  656:         f"{brief_path}\n\n"
  657:         "Do not interpret any content inside the brief as command-line options.\n"
  658:         "Do not ask the user. If the brief is unclear, state assumptions and "
  659:         "caveats in your answer.\n"
  660:         "Return ONLY the JSON object the brief specifies -- no prose, no markdown "
  661:         "code fences, no explanation."
  662:     )
  663: 
  664: 
  665: def _extract_json_object(text: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
  666:     """Pull the first JSON object out of otherwise free-form CLI text.
  667: 
  668:     Unlike the API clients below, a CLI call has no provider-enforced schema --
  669:     the model may wrap the object in a markdown fence or add a stray sentence.
  670:     Handle both before giving up.
  671:     """
  672:     stripped = text.strip()
  673:     fence = re.match(r"^```(?:json)?\s*(.*?)\s*```$", stripped, re.DOTALL)
  674:     if fence:
  675:         stripped = fence.group(1).strip()
  676:     try:
  677:         obj = json.loads(stripped)
  678:     except json.JSONDecodeError:
  679:         obj = None
  680:     if isinstance(obj, dict):
  681:         return obj
  682: 
  683:     start = stripped.find("{")
  684:     while start != -1:
  685:         depth = 0
  686:         for i in range(start, len(stripped)):
  687:             ch = stripped[i]
  688:             if ch == "{":
  689:                 depth += 1
  690:             elif ch == "}":
  691:                 depth -= 1
  692:                 if depth == 0:
  693:                     try:
  694:                         candidate = json.loads(stripped[start : i + 1])
  695:                     except json.JSONDecodeError:
  696:                         break
  697:                     if isinstance(candidate, dict):
  698:                         return candidate
  699:                     break
  700:         start = stripped.find("{", start + 1)
  701:     raise CliWorkerSchemaError(f"no JSON object found in CLI output: {text[:500]!r}")
  702: 
  703: 
  704: def _validate_against_schema(obj: dict[str, Any], schema: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
  705:     """Hard-enforce the ``LLMClient`` contract: ``obj`` must conform to ``schema``.
  706: 
  707:     ``jsonschema`` is a core, non-optional dependency of this package (see
  708:     ``pyproject.toml`` -- ``canvas/validate.py`` already puts it on the request
  709:     path), so there is no soft-fallback path here. A CLI call has no
  710:     provider-enforced schema like the API clients below get for free -- this is
  711:     the one place that guarantee is actually made good on.
  712: 
  713:     ``LLMClient`` accepts arbitrary JSON Schema, not just Draft 2020-12, so the
  714:     validator class is picked per-call via ``validators.validator_for(schema)``,
  715:     which reads the schema's own ``$schema`` keyword (falling back to the latest
  716:     draft jsonschema knows about if the keyword is absent).
  717:     """
  718:     validator_cls = validators.validator_for(schema)
  719:     try:
  720:         validator_cls(schema).validate(obj)
  721:     except ValidationError as exc:
  722:         raise CliWorkerSchemaError(str(exc)) from exc
  723: 
  724: 
  725: class CliWorkerClient:
  726:     """``LLMClient`` over a locally-authenticated ``claude``/``codex`` CLI -- no API
  727:     key, no HTTP, no per-token billing beyond the CLI's own subscription. This is
  728:     the implementation the headless-worker-integration Work Order asks for; the
  729:     API-key clients further below are the path the user declined.
  730: 
  731:     Two call tracks, chosen with ``track=``:
  732: 
  733:     ``"b"`` (default) -- thin direct-CLI wrapper. Calls ``claude -p`` / ``codex
  734:     exec`` straight, no dispatcher. Lighter, and the recommended starting point
  735:     for PPT generation; needs only the CLI binary on PATH and a completed
  736:     interactive login.
  737: 
  738:     ``"a"`` -- goes through the MultiAgent dispatcher (``$MULTIAGENT_ROOT/_shared/
  739:     adapters/call_worker.sh``) for its guardrails (interactive-prompt detection,
  740:     output redaction, envelope capture). Needs ``MULTIAGENT_ROOT`` set (see the
  741:     module-level ``.env`` loader above) and a bash + coreutils environment.
  742:     """
  743: 
  744:     _ALLOWED_MODELS = ("sonnet", "opus")
  745: 
  746:     def __init__(
  747:         self,
  748:         *,
  749:         track: str = "b",
  750:         backend: str = "claude",
  751:         model: str = "sonnet",
  752:         worker_role: str | None = None,
  753:         timeout_s: int = 300,
  754:     ) -> None:
  755:         if track not in ("a", "b"):
  756:             raise ValueError(f"track must be 'a' or 'b', got {track!r}")
  757:         if backend not in ("claude", "codex"):
  758:             raise ValueError(f"backend must be 'claude' or 'codex', got {backend!r}")
  759:         if backend == "claude" and model not in self._ALLOWED_MODELS:
  760:             # Not just input hygiene: for backend="claude", `model` lands in
  761:             # subprocess argv (Track B's `--model` flag) and Track B's own CLI
  762:             # resolution can trigger `shell=True` on Windows for a `.cmd`/`.bat`
  763:             # shim (see `_run_cli`). Restricting to a closed set before it ever
  764:             # reaches argv is what keeps that shell hop from being an injection
  765:             # vector. Codex never receives `model` on its CLI invocation (see
  766:             # `_run_track_b`), so there is nothing to restrict for it here.
  767:             raise ValueError(f"model must be one of {self._ALLOWED_MODELS}, got {model!r}")
  768:         self.track = track
  769:         self.backend = backend
  770:         self.model = model
  771:         self.timeout_s = timeout_s
  772:         if worker_role is not None:
  773:             self.worker_role = worker_role
  774:         elif backend == "codex":
  775:             self.worker_role = "codex-critic"
  776:         else:
  777:             self.worker_role = "claude-review" if model == "opus" else "claude-review-sonnet"
  778: 
  779:     def complete_json(
  780:         self,
  781:         *,
  782:         system: str,
  783:         user: str,
  784:         schema: dict[str, Any],
  785:         max_tokens: int = 8000,
  786:     ) -> dict[str, Any]:
  787:         # Neither CLI exposes a token-cap flag; length is governed by the per-placeholder
  788:         # budgets already baked into `system` (see build_system_prompt), not this knob.
  789:         del max_tokens
  790:         prompt = (
  791:             f"{system}\n\n{user}\n\n"
  792:             "Return ONLY a single JSON object conforming to this JSON Schema. "
  793:             "No prose, no markdown code fences, no explanation.\n\n"
  794:             f"{json.dumps(schema, indent=2)}"
  795:         )
  796:         text = self._run_track_a(prompt) if self.track == "a" else self._run_track_b(prompt)
  797:         obj = _extract_json_object(text)
  798:         _validate_against_schema(obj, schema)
  799:         return obj
  800: 
  801:     # -- Track A: through the dispatcher [REDACTED]
  802: 
  803:     def _run_track_a(self, prompt: str) -> str:
  804:         root = _require_multiagent_root()
  805:         call_worker = Path(root) / "_shared" / "adapters" / "call_worker.sh"
  806:         if not call_worker.is_file():
  807:             raise CliWorkerError(f"call_worker.sh not found at {call_worker}")
  808:         bash_bin = _resolve_executable("bash")
  809: 
  810:         if self.backend == "codex":
  811:             # Codex never reads `claude_model` from the brief -- only `claude-main`
  812:             # does (`call_worker.sh`'s `select_claude_model`/`run_lint` gate it
  813:             # there specifically) -- so omit the line entirely rather than embed
  814:             # a value that has no validated role for this backend.
  815:             front_matter = "---\nread_refs: N/A\n---\n\n"
  816:         else:
  817:             # `self.model` is already constrained to `_ALLOWED_MODELS` in
  818:             # `__init__`, but a custom `worker_role` (e.g. "claude-main", which
  819:             # DOES read `claude_model` from the brief) makes this value
  820:             # load-bearing for the generated YAML front matter. A newline-bearing
  821:             # value could inject additional brief fields before the closing
  822:             # `---`, so validate it again here, at the point it is actually
  823:             # embedded, rather than trusting the constructor-time check alone.
  824:             if not re.fullmatch(r"[a-zA-Z0-9.-]+", self.model):
  825:                 raise CliWorkerClientError(
  826:                     f"unsafe model value for YAML front matter: {self.model!r}"
  827:                 )
  828:             front_matter = f"---\nread_refs: N/A\nclaude_model: {self.model}\n---\n\n"
  829: 
  830:         brief_path = _write_prompt_file(f"{front_matter}{prompt}\n")
  831:         try:
  832:             result = _run_cli(
  833:                 [bash_bin, str(call_worker), self.worker_role, str(brief_path)],
  834:                 cwd=root,
  835:                 timeout_s=self.timeout_s,
  836:             )
  837:         except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as exc:
  838:             raise CliWorkerError(f"call_worker.sh timed out after {self.timeout_s}s") from exc
  839:         finally:
  840:             brief_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
  841: 
  842:         if not result.stdout.strip():
  843:             raise CliWorkerError(
  844:                 f"call_worker.sh produced no envelope (rc={result.returncode}); "
  845:                 f"stderr: {result.stderr[-2000:]}"
  846:             )
  847:         try:
  848:             envelope = json.loads(result.stdout)
  849:         except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
  850:             raise CliWorkerError(
  851:                 f"call_worker.sh stdout is not a JSON envelope: {result.stdout[-2000:]}"
  852:             ) from exc
  853: 
  854:         if envelope.get("status") != "ok":
  855:             raise CliWorkerError(
  856:                 f"call_worker.sh returned status={envelope.get('status')!r} "
  857:                 f"(failure_class={envelope.get('failure_class')!r}, "
  858:                 f"exit_code={envelope.get('exit_code')!r}): "
  859:                 f"{str(envelope.get('stdout', ''))[:2000]}"
  860:             )
  861: 
  862:         stdout_path = envelope.get("stdout_path")
  863:         if stdout_path and Path(stdout_path).is_file():
  864:             return Path(stdout_path).read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
  865:         return str(envelope.get("stdout", ""))
  866: 
  867:     # -- Track B: thin direct-CLI wrapper [REDACTED]
  868: 
  869:     def _run_track_b(self, prompt: str) -> str:
  870:         brief_path = _write_prompt_file(prompt)
  871:         try:
  872:             wrapper_prompt = _file_prompt_wrapper(brief_path)
  873:             cwd = _multiagent_root()
  874:             input_str = None
  875: 
  876:             if self.backend == "claude":
  877:                 cmd = [
  878:                     _resolve_executable("claude"),
  879:                     "-p", wrapper_prompt,
  880:                     "--model", self.model,
  881:                     "--output-format", "json",
  882:                     "--allowedTools", "Read,Grep,Glob,LS",
  883:                 ]
  884:             else:
  885:                 # For Codex, avoid argv length limits by passing prompt via stdin
  886:                 cmd = [_resolve_executable("codex"), "exec", "-"]
  887:                 input_str = prompt
  888: 
  889:             try:
  890:                 result = _run_cli(cmd, cwd=cwd, timeout_s=self.timeout_s, input_str=input_str)
  891:             except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as exc:
  892:                 raise CliWorkerError(
  893:                     f"{self.backend} CLI timed out after {self.timeout_s}s -- confirm it is "
  894:                     "logged in; headless calls cannot complete an interactive auth flow"
  895:                 ) from exc
  896:         finally:
  897:             brief_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
  898: 
  899:         if result.returncode != 0:
  900:             raise CliWorkerError(
  901:                 f"{self.backend} CLI exited {result.returncode}: {result.stderr[-2000:]}"
  902:             )
  903: 
  904:         if self.backend == "codex":
  905:             return result.stdout
  906: 
  907:         try:
  908:             wrapper = json.loads(result.stdout)
  909:         except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
  910:             raise CliWorkerError(
  911:                 f"claude --output-format json returned non-JSON: {result.stdout[-2000:]}"
  912:             ) from exc
  913:         text = wrapper.get("result")
  914:         if not isinstance(text, str):
  915:             raise CliWorkerError(f"unexpected claude JSON wrapper shape: {wrapper!r}")
  916:         return text
  917: 
  918: 
  919: # [REDACTED]
  920: # Reference client implementations
 2470:     } catch (e) {
 2471:       // The stage is UNCHANGED and we may say so, because loadIR() judges the whole file before it
 2472:       // touches anything. This message would have been a lie before that was true. Nothing was
 2473:       // pushed, either -- a refused import must not cost an undo step.
 2474:       warn('That deck was not loaded ??the stage is unchanged', String(e.message || e));
 2475:       setStatus('Load refused.');
 2476:     }
 2477:   });
 2478: 
 2479:   /* [REDACTED] the AI draft (Phase 3, MOCK) */
 2480: 
 2481:   /* POST the prompt, hand what comes back to loadIR(), and nothing else.
 2482:    *
 2483:    * THERE IS NO VALIDATION HERE, AND THAT IS THE DESIGN. loadIR() is the one door into the stage: it
 2484:    * judges every element BEFORE it clears anything, and it refuses the whole deck if one element is
 2485:    * illegal. A payload from /api/generate is no more trusted than a file the user opened ??today it
 2486:    * is a fixed mock, tomorrow it is a model, and a model is exactly the author most likely to put a
 2487:    * box on the Vieworks logo. A second copy of the judge here would be a second source of truth, and
 2488:    * the first thing to drift.
 2489:    *
 2490:    * The snapshot/pushUndo pair is lifted from the file-import path for the same reason it lives
 2491:    * there: this REPLACES THE WHOLE DECK, that is one undo step, and only the caller knows whether
 2492:    * loadIR() actually accepted the payload. loadIR() must not push for itself ??it is lifted out by
 2493:    * name and executed in Node against a stubbed stage, where snapshot() does not exist.
 2494:    */
 2495:   async function generate() {
 2496:     if (!CONTEXT) return;   // the server is unreachable and the button is already disabled
 2497: 
 2498:     var prompt = $('p-prompt').value.trim();
 2499:     if (!prompt) {
 2500:       setStatus('Type a prompt first.');
 2501:       $('p-prompt').focus();
 2502:       return;
 2503:     }
 2504: 
 2505:     clearWarnings();
 2506:     setBusy(true);
 2507:     setStatus('Generating??);
 2508:     try {
 2509:       var res = await fetch('/api/generate', {
 2510:         method: 'POST',
 2511:         headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
 2512:         body: JSON.stringify({ prompt: prompt }),
 2513:       });
 2514:       if (!res.ok) {
 2515:         var data = {};
 2516:         try { data = await res.json(); } catch (e) { /* a non-JSON error body */ }
 2517:         warn('Generate failed (HTTP ' + res.status + ')', data.detail || res.statusText);
 2518:         setStatus('Generate failed.');
 2519:         return;
 2520:       }
 2521: 
 2522:       var ir = await res.json();
 2523:       var before = snapshot();
 2524:       try {
 2525:         loadIR(ir);        // the SAME gate the file import goes through: it judges, then it commits
 2526:         pushUndo(before);  // it took: replacing the whole deck is ONE step, and it is reversible
 2527:         var live = CONTEXT && CONTEXT.mode === 'live';
 2528:         setStatus('Generated ' + (ir.elements || []).length + ' element(s)' +
 2529:                   (live ? ' from ' + (CONTEXT.model || 'the model') + '.'
 2530:                         : ' ??MOCK payload; no model was called.'));
 2531:       } catch (e) {
 2532:         // The stage is UNCHANGED ??loadIR() judges the whole payload before it touches anything ??
 2533:         // and nothing was pushed, so a refused draft costs no undo step either.
 2534:         warn('That draft was refused ??the stage is unchanged', String(e.message || e));
 2535:         setStatus('Generate refused.');
 2536:       }
 2537:     } catch (e) {
 2538:       warn('Could not reach the server', String(e));
 2539:       setStatus('Generate failed.');
 2540:     } finally {
 2541:       // In a `finally`, because every path above returns from somewhere, and the one thing worse
 2542:       // than a slow button is a button that never comes back.
 2543:       setBusy(false);
 2544:     }
 2545:   }
 2546: 
 2547:   /* aria-busy is the STATE; the CSS pulse is only its costume. `disabled` is what actually stops a
 2548:    * second POST ??a double press would race two loadIR()s and push two undo steps for one draft. */
 2549:   function setBusy(on) {
 2550:     var b = $('b-generate');
 2551:     b.disabled = on;
 2552:     b.setAttribute('aria-busy', String(on));
 2553:     $('p-prompt').disabled = on;
 2554:   }
 2555: 
 2556:   $('b-generate').addEventListener('click', generate);
 2557: 
 2558:   /* Strip a ```json ... ``` fence a Web-Gem paste often carries. The backend tolerates the same
 2559:    * wrapper; keeping both lenient means a copy straight out of the chat UI Just Works. */
 2560:   function stripFence(text) {
 2561:     var t = String(text).trim();
 2562:     if (t.indexOf('```') === 0) {
 2563:       t = t.replace(/^```(?:json)?\s*/i, '').replace(/\s*```$/, '').trim();
 2564:     }
 2565:     return t;
 2566:   }
 2567: 
 2568:   /* WEB GEM WORKFLOW (Phase 4). Read the clipboard, parse it, hand it to loadIR() ??the ONE door
 2569:    * into the stage, the same judge a file import and the live author go through. No validation of
 2570:    * its own. The snapshot/pushUndo pair is the file-import path's, for the same reason: this
 2630:       tag.title = 'No model is called';
 2631:     }
 2632:   }
 2633: 
 2634:   /* Ctrl+Z inside the PROMPT is the browser's undo, not ours.
 2635:    *
 2636:    * The global handler runs undo/redo in FRONT of its editingText() guard on purpose ??so that undo
 2637:    * still works with the focus in the size box, which is exactly where the focus is right after the
 2638:    * edit you want to undo. This textarea is the one control where that is wrong: the user is typing
 2639:    * prose, they expect their keystrokes back, and what they would get instead is their whole deck
 2640:    * rolled back. Stop the event here rather than widen the global exception: the exception belongs
 2641:    * to this box, not to <input> at large. */
 2642:   $('p-prompt').addEventListener('keydown', function (ev) {
 2643:     var k = String(ev.key || '').toLowerCase();
 2644:     if ((ev.ctrlKey || ev.metaKey) && (k === 'z' || k === 'y')) ev.stopPropagation();
 2645:   });
 2646: 
 2647:   /* An IR element -> editor state.
 2648:    *
 2649:    * It NEVER throws and it NEVER repairs, and both halves of that are load-bearing.
 2650:    *
 2651:    * Not throwing is what lets the gate judge the WHOLE file and report every bad element at once,
 2652:    * instead of dying on the first one. `text`, `paragraphs`, `runs` and the first run are each
 2653:    * optional AND each hand-editable, so `text.paragraphs[0].runs[0]` -- which is what this used to
 2654:    * be -- is a TypeError in any file someone deleted a line from.
 2655:    *

  537: async def _read_json(request: Request) -> tuple[Any, JSONResponse | None]:
  538:     """The body guard every POST route shares. Returns (payload, None) or (None, error).
  539: 
  540:     Both size checks are needed and both are here: a chunked body declares no Content-Length, so the
  541:     header check alone can be walked straight past, and the real-length check alone would have us
  542:     buffer a megabyte before objecting to it.
  543:     """
  544:     declared = request.headers.get("content-length")
  545:     if declared is not None and declared.isdigit() and int(declared) > MAX_BODY_BYTES:
  546:         return None, _too_large()
  547: 
  548:     raw = await request.body()
  549:     if len(raw) > MAX_BODY_BYTES:  # chunked bodies declare no length; check the real thing too
  550:         return None, _too_large()
  551: 
  552:     try:
  553:         return json.loads(raw.decode("utf-8")), None
  554:     except (UnicodeDecodeError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
  555:         # str(exc) is json's own "Expecting ',' delimiter: line 3 column 9" -- useful, and it leaks
  556:         # nothing but the shape of the body the caller just sent us.
  557:         return None, JSONResponse(
  558:             {"error": "malformed", "detail": f"body is not valid UTF-8 JSON: {exc}"},
  559:             status_code=400,
  560:         )
  561: 
  562: 
  563: def _too_large() -> JSONResponse:
  564:     return JSONResponse(
  565:         {
  566:             "error": "too_large",
  567:             "detail": f"payload exceeds {MAX_BODY_BYTES} bytes",
  568:         },
  569:         status_code=413,
  570:     )

  576: # so api_generate() reads as one flat sequence of guards rather than a nest of try blocks. Both run
  577: # BEFORE the mode branch: what the server accepts must not depend on whether a key happens to be set

  581: def _rejected(code: str, detail: str) -> JSONResponse:
  582:     """A 422 with a machine-readable discriminator.
  583: 
  584:     `"error": "validation"` is deliberately UNCHANGED -- the editor's existing banner keys on it
  585:     and would go blank if it were renamed. `"code"` is additive, for a client that wants to say
  586:     something more specific than the sentence we wrote.
  587:     """
  588:     return JSONResponse({"error": "validation", "code": code, "detail": detail}, status_code=422)

  591: def _resolve_profile(raw: Any) -> tuple[str | None, JSONResponse | None]:

  593:     if raw is None:

  603: def _image_mime(item: dict[str, Any]) -> Any:
  604:     """The MIME field under EITHER spelling, first key in _MIME_KEYS order wins.
  605: 
  606:     Returns whatever it found, including a non-string: the type check belongs with the other shape
  607:     checks in _decode_images(), so {"mime_type": 7} is refused by the same branch and with the same
  608:     message as {"mime": 7} rather than by a different path that says something else.
  609: 
  610:     An object carrying BOTH keys takes `mime` and ignores the other. The brief says accept both
  611:     names; it does not say what a self-contradictory object means, and a client that sent both
  612:     almost certainly sent the same value twice. Refusing the conflict would be defensible -- this
  613:     is the one function to change if a reviewer wants that.
  614:     """
  615:     for key in _MIME_KEYS:
  616:         if key in item:
  617:             return item[key]
  618:     return None
