  621: def _decode_images(raw: Any) -> tuple[list[dict[str, Any]] | None, JSONResponse | None]:
  622:     """Base64 image objects -> decoded bytes IN MEMORY, or a 422 naming the first thing wrong.
  623: 
  624:     NOTHING IS WRITTEN TO DISK. The decoded bytes live in the returned list, go into a
  625:     types.Part.from_bytes() and are collected when the request ends. There is no temp file, no
  626:     upload directory and no filename taken from the body -- the same rule /api/export has always
  627:     followed, for the same reason.
  628:     """
  629:     if raw is None:
  630:         return [], None
  631: 
  632:     if not isinstance(raw, list):
  633:         return None, _rejected(
  634:             "images_shape",
  635:             "'images' must be a list of {\"mime\": \"image/png\", \"data\": \"<base64>\"} objects",
  636:         )
  637:     if len(raw) > MAX_IMAGES:
  638:         return None, _rejected(
  639:             "too_many_images",
  640:             f"{len(raw)} images attached; at most {MAX_IMAGES} are accepted",
  641:         )
  642: 
  643:     decoded: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
  644:     total = 0
  645:     for i, item in enumerate(raw):
  646:         if not isinstance(item, dict):
  647:             return None, _rejected(
  648:                 "images_shape", f"images[{i}]: expected an object, got {type(item).__name__}"
  649:             )
  650: 
  651:         mime = _image_mime(item)
  652:         data = item.get("data")
  653:         if not isinstance(mime, str) or not isinstance(data, str):
  654:             return None, _rejected(
  655:                 "images_shape",
  656:                 f"images[{i}]: 'mime' (or 'mime_type') and 'data' must both be strings",
  657:             )
  658: 
  659:         # "image/png; charset=binary" is a browser being thorough, not a different type. Normalise,
  660:         # then match by EXACT membership.
  661:         normalised = mime.split(";")[0].strip().lower()
  662:         if normalised not in IMAGE_MIME_TYPES:
  663:             return None, _rejected(
  664:                 "unsupported_mime",
  665:                 f"images[{i}]: {mime!r} is not one of {list(IMAGE_MIME_TYPES)}",
  666:             )
  667: 
  668:         try:
  669:             blob = base64.b64decode(data, validate=True)
  670:         except (binascii.Error, ValueError) as exc:
  671:             # BOTH exception types matter. validate=True governs only binascii's alphabet check; the
  672:             # str -> ASCII coercion that runs BEFORE it raises a plain ValueError for any non-ASCII
  673:             # character. Catching binascii.Error alone lets "?쒓?" through unhandled, and Starlette
  674:             # turns that into a 500 -- a server error for a caller's malformed field.
  675:             return None, _rejected(
  676:                 "malformed_base64", f"images[{i}]: 'data' is not valid base64: {exc}"
  677:             )
  678:         if not blob:
  679:             return None, _rejected("images_shape", f"images[{i}]: 'data' decoded to zero bytes")
  680: 
  681:         total += len(blob)
  682:         if total > MAX_IMAGE_BYTES:
  683:             # Reported on the RUNNING TOTAL, not on the image that crossed it -- naming one file
  684:             # would send someone off to shrink a picture that was never individually the problem.
  685:             return None, _rejected(
  686:                 "images_too_large",
  687:                 f"the attached images decode to more than {MAX_IMAGE_BYTES} bytes in total",
  688:             )
  689: 
  690:         decoded.append({"mime": normalised, "data": blob})
  691: 
  692:     return decoded, None
  693: 
  694: 
  695: def _validate_design_name(raw: Any) -> tuple[str | None, JSONResponse | None]:
  696:     """A design name safe to use as a filename stem, or a 422. AI_PPT_v2 Phase 4.
  697: 
  698:     The SAME two-tuple shape every validator in this section returns. `.` and `..` already pass
  699:     _SAFE_DESIGN_NAME (both are made only of characters the regex allows) and are refused BY NAME
  700:     here instead -- _design_path()'s containment check would also catch either one, but refusing it
  701:     here gives the caller a readable reason instead of a generic 500.
  702: 
  703:     WINDOWS-RESERVED DEVICE NAMES ARE REFUSED TOO, case-insensitively, extension or not. `CON`,
  704:     `NUL.json`, `com1`, `Lpt3.bak` -- all of these name a device file, not a data file, on Windows;
  705:     writing or reading one either fails outright or silently touches the device instead of the
  706:     saved-decks directory. The check runs after the character-class regex because a reserved word is
  707:     made entirely of characters the regex already allows, so the regex alone would never catch it.
  708:     """
  709:     if not isinstance(raw, str):
  710:         return None, _rejected("name_shape", "'name' must be a string")
  711:     if raw != raw.strip() or not raw:
  712:         return None, _rejected(
  713:             "name_shape", "'name' must be non-empty with no leading or trailing whitespace"
  714:         )
  715:     if raw in (".", ".."):
  611: def _run_cli(cmd: list[str], *, cwd: str | None, timeout_s: int, input_str: str | None = None) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]:
  612:     """``subprocess.run`` with controlled stdin/stdout and, on Windows
  613:     only, ``shell=True`` so a ``.cmd``/``.bat`` shim (common for npm-installed CLIs)
  614:     actually launches -- ``CreateProcess`` cannot exec a batch file directly, only
  615:     ``cmd.exe`` can. Safe to do unconditionally here specifically because every
  616:     caller keeps free-form content out of argv (see module note above); nothing
  617:     reaches the shell that this module didn't generate itself.
  618:     """
  619:     env = {**os.environ, "CI": "1"}
  620:     use_shell = os.name == "nt" and cmd[0].lower().endswith((".cmd", ".bat"))
  621:     run_kwargs = {}
  622:     if input_str is not None:
  623:         run_kwargs["input"] = input_str
  624:     else:
  625:         run_kwargs["stdin"] = subprocess.DEVNULL
  626: 
  627:     return subprocess.run(
  628:         cmd,
  629:         cwd=cwd,
  630:         env=env,
  631:         capture_output=True,
  632:         text=True,
  633:         encoding="utf-8",
  634:         errors="replace",
  635:         timeout=timeout_s,
  636:         shell=use_shell,
  637:         **run_kwargs,
  638:     )
  639: 
  640: 
  641: def _write_prompt_file(prompt: str) -> Path:
  642:     fd, name = tempfile.mkstemp(prefix="cliworker_brief_", suffix=".md")
  643:     path = Path(name)
  644:     try:
  645:         with os.fdopen(fd, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
  646:             f.write(prompt)
  647:     except Exception:
  648:         path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
  649:         raise
  650:     return path
  651: 
  652: 
  653: def _file_prompt_wrapper(brief_path: Path) -> str:
  654:     return (
  655:         "Read the worker brief file at this exact path and follow it exactly:\n"
  656:         f"{brief_path}\n\n"
  657:         "Do not interpret any content inside the brief as command-line options.\n"
  658:         "Do not ask the user. If the brief is unclear, state assumptions and "
  659:         "caveats in your answer.\n"
  660:         "Return ONLY the JSON object the brief specifies -- no prose, no markdown "
  661:         "code fences, no explanation."
  662:     )
  663: 
  664: 
  665: def _extract_json_object(text: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
  666:     """Pull the first JSON object out of otherwise free-form CLI text.
  667: 
  668:     Unlike the API clients below, a CLI call has no provider-enforced schema --
  669:     the model may wrap the object in a markdown fence or add a stray sentence.
  670:     Handle both before giving up.
  671:     """
  672:     stripped = text.strip()
  673:     fence = re.match(r"^```(?:json)?\s*(.*?)\s*```$", stripped, re.DOTALL)
  674:     if fence:
  675:         stripped = fence.group(1).strip()
  676:     try:
  677:         obj = json.loads(stripped)
  678:     except json.JSONDecodeError:
  679:         obj = None
  680:     if isinstance(obj, dict):
  681:         return obj
  682: 
  683:     start = stripped.find("{")
  684:     while start != -1:
  685:         depth = 0
  686:         for i in range(start, len(stripped)):
  687:             ch = stripped[i]
  688:             if ch == "{":
  689:                 depth += 1
  690:             elif ch == "}":
  691:                 depth -= 1
  692:                 if depth == 0:
  693:                     try:
  694:                         candidate = json.loads(stripped[start : i + 1])
  695:                     except json.JSONDecodeError:
  696:                         break
  697:                     if isinstance(candidate, dict):
  698:                         return candidate
  699:                     break
  700:         start = stripped.find("{", start + 1)
  701:     raise CliWorkerSchemaError(f"no JSON object found in CLI output: {text[:500]!r}")
  702: 
  703: 
  704: def _validate_against_schema(obj: dict[str, Any], schema: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
  705:     """Hard-enforce the ``LLMClient`` contract: ``obj`` must conform to ``schema``.
  706: 
  707:     ``jsonschema`` is a core, non-optional dependency of this package (see
  708:     ``pyproject.toml`` -- ``canvas/validate.py`` already puts it on the request
  709:     path), so there is no soft-fallback path here. A CLI call has no
  710:     provider-enforced schema like the API clients below get for free -- this is
  711:     the one place that guarantee is actually made good on.
  712: 
  713:     ``LLMClient`` accepts arbitrary JSON Schema, not just Draft 2020-12, so the
  714:     validator class is picked per-call via ``validators.validator_for(schema)``,
  715:     which reads the schema's own ``$schema`` keyword (falling back to the latest
  716:     draft jsonschema knows about if the keyword is absent).
  717:     """
  718:     validator_cls = validators.validator_for(schema)
  719:     try:
  720:         validator_cls(schema).validate(obj)
  721:     except ValidationError as exc:
  722:         raise CliWorkerSchemaError(str(exc)) from exc
  723: 
  724: 
  725: class CliWorkerClient:
  726:     """``LLMClient`` over a locally-authenticated ``claude``/``codex`` CLI -- no API
  727:     key, no HTTP, no per-token billing beyond the CLI's own subscription. This is
  728:     the implementation the headless-worker-integration Work Order asks for; the
  729:     API-key clients further below are the path the user declined.
  730: 
  779:     def complete_json(
  780:         self,
  781:         *,
  782:         system: str,
  783:         user: str,
  784:         schema: dict[str, Any],
  785:         max_tokens: int = 8000,
  786:     ) -> dict[str, Any]:
  787:         # Neither CLI exposes a token-cap flag; length is governed by the per-placeholder
  788:         # budgets already baked into `system` (see build_system_prompt), not this knob.
  789:         del max_tokens
  790:         prompt = (
  791:             f"{system}\n\n{user}\n\n"
  792:             "Return ONLY a single JSON object conforming to this JSON Schema. "
  793:             "No prose, no markdown code fences, no explanation.\n\n"
  794:             f"{json.dumps(schema, indent=2)}"
  795:         )
  796:         text = self._run_track_a(prompt) if self.track == "a" else self._run_track_b(prompt)
  797:         obj = _extract_json_object(text)
  798:         _validate_against_schema(obj, schema)
  799:         return obj
  800: 
  801:     # -- Track A: through the dispatcher [REDACTED]
  802: 
  803:     def _run_track_a(self, prompt: str) -> str:
  804:         root = _require_multiagent_root()
  805:         call_worker = Path(root) / "_shared" / "adapters" / "call_worker.sh"
  806:         if not call_worker.is_file():
  807:             raise CliWorkerError(f"call_worker.sh not found at {call_worker}")
  808:         bash_bin = _resolve_executable("bash")
  809: 
  810:         if self.backend == "codex":
  811:             # Codex never reads `claude_model` from the brief -- only `claude-main`
  812:             # does (`call_worker.sh`'s `select_claude_model`/`run_lint` gate it
  813:             # there specifically) -- so omit the line entirely rather than embed
  814:             # a value that has no validated role for this backend.
  815:             front_matter = "---\nread_refs: N/A\n---\n\n"
  816:         else:
  817:             # `self.model` is already constrained to `_ALLOWED_MODELS` in
  818:             # `__init__`, but a custom `worker_role` (e.g. "claude-main", which
  819:             # DOES read `claude_model` from the brief) makes this value
  820:             # load-bearing for the generated YAML front matter. A newline-bearing
  821:             # value could inject additional brief fields before the closing
  822:             # `---`, so validate it again here, at the point it is actually
  823:             # embedded, rather than trusting the constructor-time check alone.
  824:             if not re.fullmatch(r"[a-zA-Z0-9.-]+", self.model):
  825:                 raise CliWorkerClientError(
  826:                     f"unsafe model value for YAML front matter: {self.model!r}"
  827:                 )
  828:             front_matter = f"---\nread_refs: N/A\nclaude_model: {self.model}\n---\n\n"
  829: 
  830:         brief_path = _write_prompt_file(f"{front_matter}{prompt}\n")
  831:         try:
  832:             result = _run_cli(
  833:                 [bash_bin, str(call_worker), self.worker_role, str(brief_path)],
  834:                 cwd=root,
  835:                 timeout_s=self.timeout_s,
  836:             )
  837:         except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as exc:
  838:             raise CliWorkerError(f"call_worker.sh timed out after {self.timeout_s}s") from exc
  839:         finally:
  840:             brief_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
  841: 
  842:         if not result.stdout.strip():
  843:             raise CliWorkerError(
  844:                 f"call_worker.sh produced no envelope (rc={result.returncode}); "
  845:                 f"stderr: {result.stderr[-2000:]}"
  846:             )
  847:         try:
  848:             envelope = json.loads(result.stdout)
  849:         except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
  850:             raise CliWorkerError(
  851:                 f"call_worker.sh stdout is not a JSON envelope: {result.stdout[-2000:]}"
  852:             ) from exc
  853: 
  854:         if envelope.get("status") != "ok":
  855:             raise CliWorkerError(
  856:                 f"call_worker.sh returned status={envelope.get('status')!r} "
  857:                 f"(failure_class={envelope.get('failure_class')!r}, "
  858:                 f"exit_code={envelope.get('exit_code')!r}): "
  859:                 f"{str(envelope.get('stdout', ''))[:2000]}"
  860:             )
  861: 
  862:         stdout_path = envelope.get("stdout_path")
  863:         if stdout_path and Path(stdout_path).is_file():
  864:             return Path(stdout_path).read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
  865:         return str(envelope.get("stdout", ""))
  866: 
  867:     # -- Track B: thin direct-CLI wrapper [REDACTED]
  868: 
  869:     def _run_track_b(self, prompt: str) -> str:
  870:         brief_path = _write_prompt_file(prompt)
  871:         try:
  872:             wrapper_prompt = _file_prompt_wrapper(brief_path)
  873:             cwd = _multiagent_root()
  874:             input_str = None
  875: 
  876:             if self.backend == "claude":
  877:                 cmd = [
  878:                     _resolve_executable("claude"),
  879:                     "-p", wrapper_prompt,
  880:                     "--model", self.model,
  881:                     "--output-format", "json",
  882:                     "--allowedTools", "Read,Grep,Glob,LS",
  883:                 ]
  884:             else:
  885:                 # For Codex, avoid argv length limits by passing prompt via stdin
  886:                 cmd = [_resolve_executable("codex"), "exec", "-"]
  887:                 input_str = prompt
  888: 
  889:             try:
  890:                 result = _run_cli(cmd, cwd=cwd, timeout_s=self.timeout_s, input_str=input_str)
  891:             except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as exc:
  892:                 raise CliWorkerError(
  893:                     f"{self.backend} CLI timed out after {self.timeout_s}s -- confirm it is "
  894:                     "logged in; headless calls cannot complete an interactive auth flow"
  895:                 ) from exc
  896:         finally:
  897:             brief_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
  898: 
  899:         if result.returncode != 0:
  900:             raise CliWorkerError(
  901:                 f"{self.backend} CLI exited {result.returncode}: {result.stderr[-2000:]}"
  902:             )
  903: 
  904:         if self.backend == "codex":
  905:             return result.stdout
  906: 
  907:         try:
  908:             wrapper = json.loads(result.stdout)
  909:         except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
  910:             raise CliWorkerError(
  911:                 f"claude --output-format json returned non-JSON: {result.stdout[-2000:]}"
  912:             ) from exc
  913:         text = wrapper.get("result")
  914:         if not isinstance(text, str):
  915:             raise CliWorkerError(f"unexpected claude JSON wrapper shape: {wrapper!r}")
  916:         return text
  917:

 1482:     out.text = paragraphs ? { align: s.align, valign: s.valign, paragraphs: paragraphs } : null;
 1483:     return out;
 1484:   }

 1486:   function serialize() {
 1487:     return {
 1488:       schema: SCHEMA,
 1489:       canvas: { w: CANVAS.w, h: CANVAS.h, unit: CANVAS.unit },
 1490:       target: {
 1491:         template: 'reference/renderer_template.pptx', // informational ONLY; the server ignores it
 1492:         template_sha256: CONTEXT.template_sha256,     // from /api/context -- NEVER hard-coded
 1493:         layout_index: CONTEXT.layout_index,
 1494:       },
 1495:       title: titleString(),
 1496:       // :scope > .el is the whole z-order story AND the whole "chrome cannot be serialized" story:
 1497:       // the pseudo-elements have no node, and #title-field has no .el.
 1498:       elements: [].slice.call(stage.querySelectorAll(':scope > .el')).map(toIR),
 1499:     };
 1500:   }

 2279:       if (!navigator.clipboard || !navigator.clipboard.writeText) {
 2280:         throw new Error('navigator.clipboard is not available');

 2283:       setStatus('Copied JSON to clipboard.');
 2284:     } catch (e) {
 2285:       // Fallback for a non-secure context or an older browser: select the text and ask the
 2286:       // browser's own copy command to act on the current selection.
 2287:       try {
 2288:         ta.select();
 2289:         ta.setSelectionRange(0, ta.value.length);
 2290:         if (!document.execCommand('copy')) throw new Error('execCommand(copy) returned false');
 2291:         setStatus('Copied JSON to clipboard.');
 2292:       } catch (e2) {
 2293:         warn('Could not copy to clipboard', String(e2));

 2298:   /* Phase 6: /api/export (JSON in, .pptx out) was renamed to /api/download and now takes
 2299:    * multipart/form-data -- an "ir" field carrying the SAME JSON serialize() always produced, plus
 2300:    * one form part per distinct image/video `src` the deck references. NO "Content-Type" header is
 2301:    * set here: fetch() computes the multipart boundary itself from the FormData body, and a
 2302:    * hand-set header would carry no boundary at all and the server would fail to parse the parts. */
 2303:   function mediaFor(ir) {
 2304:     var names = [];

 2306:       if ((el.type === 'image' || el.type === 'video') && names.indexOf(el.src) < 0) names.push(el.src);
 2307:     });
 2308:     return names;
 2309:   }

# AI-Powered PPT Workflow Review

## 1. Frontend → Backend trigger

The UI provides a prompt textarea (`#p-prompt`), model dropdown (`#p-model`), and Generate button in `index.html`.

On Generate click, `editor.js`:

1. Reads and trims only `#p-prompt`.
2. Sends `POST /api/generate` with:
   ```json
   { "prompt": "..." }
   ```
3. Parses the JSON response.
4. Passes it to `loadIR(ir)`, which validates every element before replacing the canvas and adds one undo snapshot.

This does not automatically run merely when a model is selected or text is entered; it runs only after the Generate button is clicked.

Important inconsistency: the selected `#p-model` value is never read in `editor.js` and is not included in the request. Although the backend supports a `model` field and profiles (`sonnet`, `opus`, `gpt-5.6-terra`, `gpt-5.6-sol`), every current UI request omits it, so `_resolve_profile()` defaults to `sonnet`. The model picker is presently cosmetic/nonfunctional.

`interactions.js` is unrelated to AI generation. It supplies pointer drag/resize behavior only.

## 2. Worker execution and JSON delivery

`/api/generate` in `app.py` parses the HTTP JSON, validates a non-empty prompt, resolves the model profile, validates optional images, then branches on `AIPPT_LIVE_MODE`.

- Default/mock mode: no Worker or model is called. The prompt, profile, and images are deliberately ignored; a fixed canvas IR payload is returned after 1.5 seconds.
- Live mode (`AIPPT_LIVE_MODE=1`): `generate_live()` creates:
  ```python
  CliWorkerClient(
      track="b",
      backend=cfg["backend"],
      model=cfg["model"],
      timeout_s=900,
  )
  ```
  and calls `complete_json()` on a background thread.

`complete_json()` combines:

- the system instruction read from `GEM_Knowledge_API.md`,
- the user prompt,
- an instruction to return one JSON object only,
- the relaxed canvas JSON Schema.

It invokes a direct CLI integration (`track="b"`), not the MultiAgent dispatcher:

- Claude: writes the composed prompt to a temporary `.md` brief, asks Claude to read it, requests JSON output, extracts the `result` string.
- Codex: writes that temporary brief too, but actually sends the composed prompt directly on stdin to `codex exec -`; the temporary file is not used for the Codex call.

The adapter then extracts a JSON object from CLI output and validates it against the supplied schema. `app.py` finalizes the result by overwriting `schema`, `canvas`, and `target` with server-controlled values from the pinned template, adds a default title/elements list where needed, and applies two narrow fallbacks (connector arrow and missing run color). It validates the final IR again before returning it as the HTTP JSON response.

There is no durable record of the generated JSON in this live path:

- the temporary prompt brief is deleted in `finally`;
- CLI output is held in process memory;
- the finalized IR is returned to the browser;
- the browser retains it only as the editor’s in-memory canvas state.

The unused Track A path can consume dispatcher envelopes and their `stdout_path`, but the actual app creates Track B clients, so that recording path is not active.

A second major gap: images are accepted and validated by `/api/generate`, but `generate_live()` explicitly discards them before calling `CliWorkerClient`. Thus AI image attachments do not reach either Claude or Codex.

## 3. Slide-generation workflow

The architecture is a two-stage process, not “generation immediately creates a PPTX.”

```text
Generate click
  → POST /api/generate
  → mock or CLI-generated canvas-ir/1 JSON
  → browser loadIR()
  → editable in-browser canvas

Export click
  → serialize() current canvas state as canvas-ir/1
  → POST /api/export
  → Python render(ir)
  → in-memory .pptx bytes
  → browser download/save
```

The JSON handoffs are structurally sound:

- The adapter requires the model result to meet the relaxed schema.
- `app.py` injects authoritative template/canvas data rather than trusting model values.
- `app.py` validates the finalized IR before returning it.
- `loadIR()` validates all received elements before mutating the browser stage, preventing partial imports.
- `serialize()` emits the current title, template pin data, and ordered `elements` array.
- `/api/export` invokes Python `render(ir)`, which validates again and draws elements in array order into the presentation.

However, Python does not create slides as part of `/api/generate`. The generated JSON is first rendered into the browser editor; slide creation occurs only if the user later presses Export. Because export serializes the current browser state, it can contain user edits made after generation. This is appropriate for an editable-canvas workflow, but it deviates from an architecture where successful model output is immediately parsed and rendered by Python into a final presentation.

## Findings and bottlenecks

- **Model picker is disconnected.** The frontend always sends only `prompt`, so the backend always uses the default Sonnet profile.
- **Mock mode is the default.** Unless `AIPPT_LIVE_MODE=1` is set, no real Worker is invoked and the prompt has no effect.
- **AI attachments are a dead-end.** They are validated but never passed to the live CLI model.
- **No persistent generation artifact/audit trail exists in Track B.** Prompt brief and result are transient.
- **Track B bypasses the MultiAgent dispatcher.** It is a direct CLI call, so dispatcher-specific envelopes, guardrails, and output records are not part of the active app path.
- **Codex’s temporary brief is redundant.** Track B creates it but sends the prompt on stdin instead.
- **PPTX rendering is deferred.** Generation produces editable IR; exporting creates the `.pptx`.
- **The listed workflow supports one canvas slide per export.** Adding to an existing deck is a separate `/api/append_slide` operation; no automatic multi-slide generation/orchestration is visible in the reviewed path.
